# app — architecture

> Status: the detailing ended 2026-11-02, T08. Section 2 names the decisions, section 3 what to build, and the order of the code is epics E2–E4 in table 2 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md) and section 3.10.
>
> What the component does, what it does not do and what is still to be decided about it. This is the detail of its card, architecture 3.1 spec. The card stays the summary: if the detail changes what it says, the card is amended in `spec` by the same route as questions to its registers (RULES.md, Across repositories).
>
> Why it was decided so and not otherwise is in [decisions.md](decisions.md); the work plan is in [RUN.md](../RUN.md). The concepts are concepts spec. The documents and decisions of `spec` lie in `spec/docs/` and are cited with the suffix "spec".

## 1. Purpose and boundaries

**Does:**
- the app's shell (architecture 3.1 spec):
  - the account owner's sign-in through the sign-in service;
  - the choice of the learner on a shared device: a child learns under an adult's account and creates no account of their own (R06 spec; privacy 7 spec), and the same account can also hold the profile of the owner themselves as a learner (R26 spec);
  - the start of work — the learner's age and country, consent. The platform stores not the age and not the date of birth, but the age category, the country and the date when the age was given (privacy 3.3 spec). A parent's consent is a signed form, whose scan the operator checks, and before the public launch a card transaction is added to it (R116 spec; П-1 closed). That the account owner is an adult is confirmed by their statement "I am 18" with a date (R118 spec; П-6 closed). `api` does not yet accept learners from the countries of the EU, the EEA and the United Kingdom (R131 spec);
  - the learner's grade — when the module manifest names grades. The answer is stored in the learner's profile and can be a grade that the module does not name; the grade closes nothing and only places the learner in a difficulty band (architecture 3.1 spec; R49 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 11);
- three entries: "My subjects", "Skill map" and "A task from my paper" (product 4 spec). Tasks from paper are stage 5 (product 11 spec; О-11);
- the lesson player and the task screen: the canvas from the package `canvas`, answer slots, the choice of an option, the tutor's panel with the solving steps and hints, the conversation line (architecture 3.1 spec). The solving mode is "by steps" or "self": the default mode is set by the adult, for a new profile it is "by steps", and the learner switches it on a task (product 4 spec; concepts 10 spec):
  - the text of an item of a handwriting sheet `app` shows together with the options of the slots `choice` beside the sheet, not on it; the item line of a workbook is shown by the canvas (architecture 3.1 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14);
  - a lesson `app` leads by its open view: the segments in the author's order — explanations, worked examples and the lesson's tasks, which `api` gives (R58 spec). How exactly — section 3.4;
  - the first slice has three modules: olympiad maths 1–6 on a handwriting sheet, school maths of grade 3 on a workbook and short essays in English. It has no text canvas and no language modules (R41 spec; product 10 spec);
- the sending of work to reading — only on the learner's action. Work is read on "Check", and "Step done" in format v0 does not read the work: it only leads to the next step (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R69 spec; R05; section 3.3). On a pause the app only makes a button blink, without a call to AI (R69 spec; concepts 9.1 spec). An answer by a tapped choice of an option is checked without AI (architecture 4.2 spec); keyboard input and editing in the text of the statement will come in later versions of the format ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14). A verdict is given at each answer slot, and the verdict of an item is made up of them (R56 spec). An item with a rubric has no verdict: the learner gets feedback by the rubric (R04 spec, R75 spec; architecture 4.1 spec). After the check the learner sees what was read, and the tutor's marks lie on a layer of the tutor's own and do not touch the learner's work (R05 spec; architecture 4.1 spec);
- progress and the skill map — by the package `skill-map`, from ready data from `api`: both on the learner's screen and in the section for adults (architecture 3.3 spec). The reason code, why the engine chose a task, the adult sees, and the child is not told it (concepts 12 spec). The list of codes is the closed `ReasonCode` of the contract, nine values with `REASON_CODE_ROTATION` (R100, R107 spec). The child is told on their screen, in their language and in words clear for their age, that the adult sees their progress — attempts, rubric marks and repeating traps ([DPIA](../spec/docs/dpia.md), section 6, risk 16);
- the section for adults behind a PIN: profiles, tariff, own key, settings, export and deletion (architecture 3.1 spec). Deletion is confirmed by a fresh sign-in, not by the PIN (architecture 3.5 spec, 4.5 spec);
- export: `app` assembles it — from the device and from the part that `api` gives. The export format is open, in `spec` (architecture 4.5 spec; R09 spec);
- the disclosure that the learner talks with AI, not with a person (architecture 3.1 spec; privacy 5.3 spec), — to every learner. On top of the AI providers' measures, beside each reply of the tutor there is "Report a problem" for the adult, and learning materials on safe use (R123 spec; П-15 closed);
- to a learner under 18, at work with a rubric and in the section "Help", open to them from any screen, — where to turn if something is wrong: to an adult and to the helplines of their country, by name and number, without links, by a list with a date of checking. Before each sending of work that goes to AI whole — a reminder not to write names (R127 spec; П-20 closed);
- legal texts — pages of `app` (architecture 3.13 spec): the policy, the terms and the consents `app` gets from `api` by the method `LegalService.GetLegalTexts` and shows as its own pages, without external links, and for a consent, its gist in the screen's language beside the English text and a translation of the version, without force of its own, if there is one (R115, R116, R130, R141 spec). A consent names the text version that the person saw: `CreateAccount` — `privacy-policy` and `terms-of-use`, and a parent's consent, a teenager's own consent and the storage of artifacts — texts of their own (R131, R136 spec). In force since v0.15.0 of `spec` are `privacy-policy` of version 2, `terms-of-use`, `storage-consent` and the family's texts of version 1, and the operator in them is ALT EdTech LLC (R132, R137, R138, R141 spec). How — section 3.8;
- voicing of the tutor's words — for everyone: it is output, not data collection (privacy 6 spec). The card starts it with the device's speech synthesis (architecture 3.1 spec; Т-13): `app` names the voice itself and takes only a local one, by the rule of Т-13. Where the browser does not know this, voicing on the device is turned on by the adult — this is a choice of the system that the adult makes, not a disclosure for the child (R118 spec; П-22 closed), — and the text on the screen always stays. How — section 3.5. Later, with the text canvas, `app` will voice its "listen" too by the same speech synthesis — on an event of the canvas element. R36 spec names it `speak`, and `canvas` removed it together with the text canvas (the 2026-10-25 amendment to R07 canvas; sources 5.1);
- the interface in English and Russian (О-2): the learner's language is set by the adult in their profile, and the module's texts and the tutor's words stay in the module's explanation language, each block with its own `lang`. How — section 3.8;
- accessibility — WCAG 2.2 level AA and two AAA criteria. What the task screen does not meet yet — the answer by handwriting, the lines of the workbook and the sending of an answer without confirmation, — `app` names on the page "Accessibility". How — section 3.9.

**Does not:**
- does not get the key, the solution and the traps of an unsolved task. The app receives the open view, which `module-kit` builds by the marks `x-open` in the format's schemas: a field without the mark is closed (R05 spec, R45 spec; architecture 1 spec; repositories 4 spec). The task's solution comes after a correct answer or the showing of the solution (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05 spec; R82 spec). The task's open view has none of its skills, band and difficulty (R51 spec), techniques, traps, simpler case and leak phrases (R57 spec). The task's technique `app` gets only from `api` — at the step that gives it, or at the learner's request (architecture 3.1 spec; R57 spec);
- does not give a verdict: `module-kit` gives it, in the process of `api` (R03 spec; architecture 3.5 spec). The solving steps `app` leads by the open view, and the exit conditions of a step are events on the device, without the key (architecture 3.1 spec). How — section 3.3;
- does not call AI providers: everything that calls a model is in `tutor`, a library inside `api` (architecture 1 spec; R15 spec). The own key goes with the request to `api` and lives there only in the request's memory (architecture 4.3 spec), and how `app` holds and sends it is section 3.6;
- does not send strokes and the whole photo to the server. What goes to the check is an image or a crop of the work, and a photo from paper is cropped to the task on the device, before the call to AI (R09 spec; privacy 6 spec). From a workbook go the crops of the filled boxes with their places, without the item's text and the scratch work (R53 spec; architecture 4.1 spec). Of the writing history nothing goes to AI — neither the order of writing, nor the log of actions, nor the frames before erasing (R52 spec);
- does not load its parts of the interface at run time. Foreign code is only the widgets of modules, from stage 4, in the iframe sandbox, through the widget host from `canvas` (R16 spec; architecture 3.4, 6 spec);
- keeps no server process of its own: in the first slice there is one service, `api`, and the build of `app` is served by the hosting that `infra` chooses (architecture 1 spec, 3.11 spec, 3.12 spec). The address of `api` and the sign-in parameters the build does not embed: it reads them at start from a file beside itself, `/deployment.json`, — section 3.7;
- does not show public profiles, contact between learners, geolocation, ads and external links (privacy 1 spec, 7 spec); for children, no timers and competitions (product 7 spec);
- does not take voice input: it is not in the first slice (product 10 spec), and for children it comes only after the decision of П-5; the legal questions are decided by the author on Claude's analysis (R112 spec; О-6).

**Data and links:**
- on the device: strokes with time and pressure, the learner's artifacts and the own key — the key only if the adult so decided on this device (architecture 3.1 spec, 5 spec; R84 spec; section 3.6); with tasks from paper, also photos from paper (architecture 5 spec; О-11). Artifacts on the server — only if the account owner turned this on for the profile, by the consent text `storage-consent`; by default storage is off, and removing it erases the copies on the server (R113, R131 spec; П-8 closed). What lies on the device, how much and until when, is section 3.2;
- also on the device, the context of each submission: the canvas gives it together with the work, and `app` holds it as it is, and by the digest the canvas accepts the context back (architecture 3.1 spec; R36 spec). What is in it is section 3.2;
- the open views of the module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps `app` gets from `api` by the package digest of the attempt, once per package, and caches them by it, and to the canvas it gives the manifest by the property `manifest` (architecture 3.1 spec; R36 spec, R62 spec). The open views of a withdrawn version of a module it removes from the cache (R63 spec). Media files come from the device part, which `api` serves (architecture 3.10 spec; R62 spec);
- on a shared device several learners learn under one sign-in of the account owner, and the owner themselves can be among them (architecture 3.1 spec; R06 spec, R26 spec). The owner profile is closed to a child by the same PIN as the section for adults (R84 spec; section 3.6). A teenager's account is П-13;
- only `api` stores learners' data on the server (architecture 1 spec). The learner's profile is the pseudonym, the avatar, the age category, the country and the date the age was given, the presentation style with voicing, the default mode, the interface language, the grade — if the module asks for it, — and for a child also the adult's setting "ask before showing" the solution (privacy 4 spec; concepts 13 spec; R82 spec). The profile's fields in the contract are `AccountService`; there are no interests there until the format gives a framing of a task by interests (R98 spec);
- talks only with `api` and with the sign-in service (architecture 3.1 spec; repositories 2.1 spec). The sign-in service — Firebase Auth through Google (R03 api) — has the addresses of Google that section 3.7 names. The API contracts are protobuf and buf (R11 spec): proto3, over Connect, by unary calls only, the client in `app` is connect-es, and the TypeScript code is the npm package `@altnovatrail/contracts` from the release of `spec`. Within a major package an old build of `app` works with a new `api` (R83 spec). The module format is JSON Schema 2020-12 (R45 spec): Т-7 closed. With requests to platform AI `app` sends the device's clock offset from UTC, in minutes: by it the family day of the quota ends, and this is not geolocation but account data (R118 spec; П-23 closed; concepts 14 spec). The contract `app` ↔ `api` is the package `novatrail.api.v1`: the check of an attempt is `AttemptService` (R97 spec), the account and profiles are `AccountService` (R98 spec), the path from a module to a task is `PackageService`, `ModuleService`, `LearningService` and `ProgressService` (R99 spec), the legal texts are `LegalService` (R130 spec). A refusal is a Connect error with a standard code and the detail `Refusal`, and the AI call outcome is a field of the successful response (R97 spec); a place on the canvas in it is in shares 0–1000 of the crop (R53 spec). What each screen takes from the contract is sections 3.2–3.4 and 3.6. The tutor's words come to the device whole, when the whole text has passed the leak check and the form check, not as a stream (R54 spec, R70 spec). There is no real-time channel in the first slice (architecture 7 spec; Т-25);
- the task's open view `app` reads by the TypeScript types from the npm package `@altnovatrail/spec`, `@altnovatrail/spec/formatv0/open`, and `api` reads the same view by the Go types (R72 spec, R73 spec). While the format is v0, the platform reads only its current version, and `app` and `api` move to a new version together (R44 spec; architecture 3.1 spec; repositories 4 spec);
- for a review of the course of the solution, sends the work again: the server does not store the image (architecture 4.1 spec);
- at build time depends only on `canvas` and `skill-map`, besides the packages of `spec` — `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` (R73, R83 spec), — and the icon set Material Symbols (R78 spec): it builds them in by exact version, and in development it connects their working copies (repositories 2.1 spec, 3 spec; R16 spec; architecture 6 spec). The packages enter the app as web components, `canvas` as the element `<novatrail-canvas>` (R36 spec), and are installed from npm, from the scope `@altnovatrail` (R37 spec). The signature of their version is checked by the recipe of `spec` `release-verify` — for an npm lock file, its mode `release-verify-npm` — in CI of the pull request that changes the lock file (architecture 3.1 spec; R77 spec; RULES.md, Hard rules). The types of the canvas element are `@altnovatrail/spec/canvas`, in the same package of types (R74 spec). The package `@altnovatrail/spec` lies in `devDependencies`, by an exact version — the same as the tag of the submodule `spec/` (R73 spec; repositories 1 spec);
- the build is rolled out by `infra` by the same list of exact versions and digests as the services (R106 spec; Т-19 closed), and the hosting of the build and the domains are with `infra` too (architecture 3.11 spec). The hosting is Firebase Hosting: Google is a processor for it, the link of Hosting with Cloud Logging is turned off, and its period — "a few months" — stands in the written retention policy (R128 spec; П-9 closed). The platform's data region is the USA (R109 spec);
- the license is Apache-2.0; dependencies only with licenses compatible with it (R12 spec; R20 spec);
- the screens are described in [the prototype's README](../spec/draft/README.md) (product 4 spec); where the prototype differs from the documents, the documents prevail;
- stage 2 — the first slice with a learner, together with `tutor`, `canvas`, `api`, `skill-map` and `infra` (product 11 spec; repositories 2.1 spec). Before the first child: the parent's verifiable consent (R116 spec), the contracts with the AI providers (R121 spec) and the DPIA (R119, R129 spec; repositories 6 spec; product 10 spec);
- before the slice, the first app: the first build of `app` with `api`, on which the author solves tasks themselves in the browser, locally — with handwriting, lessons and the engine, with sign-in, without children and without deployment. Tasks in it come by lessons, and there is no own key and no tariffs. The order of the tasks of all repositories toward it is led by the cross-repository plan of `spec`, [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md) (R76 spec; repositories 6 spec).

## 2. To decide when detailed

The items "To decide when detailed" of the card — a subsection per item. Each is decided by a task of its own of epic E1 in [RUN.md](../RUN.md). A decided item names the entry of the log, and the decision itself moves to section 3. A question that goes beyond this repository goes «в реестр spec» (RULES.md, Across repositories).

The card has eight items. Subsections 2.1–2.7 are the seven it named at `spec` v0.2.9, in its order. The lesson player the card got later and placed fourth (R58 spec), and here it is subsection 2.8: the log and the tasks already cite the numbers 2.4–2.7 (R01). The interface languages and accessibility the card gives as one item, and it is one subsection, 2.7. There are no items beyond the card. The task that set up the repository names the same items, except accessibility and the lesson player (T12 `spec`): accessibility the card puts in one item with the languages, and the lesson player was not yet in it then.

Eight more questions of `spec` concern the screens of `app`, though they did not become items of the card; of them, О-7 and О-21 were closed by R82 spec, and П-20 by R127 spec:
- ranks and the persona for adults — О-13, whose column "Blocks" has `app`;
- the button that shows the task's solution — О-7, closed by R82 spec: to an adult from the start of the attempt, to a child from the fourth wrong answer, with the adult's setting "ask before showing";
- the tariff and the platform AI quota in the section for adults — О-14. In the first slice platform AI is without payment, within the quota (product 10 spec): in dollars per family day, with caps per profile and per account, and the learner and the adult see the quota share, not the sum (concepts 14 spec);
- the mode without AI — a free path, open to children too (О-5; product 9 spec);
- a hint at the learner's request — О-21, closed by R82 spec: before the first wrong answer the request gives "givens and unknown" and the task's technique, after it — the words of the current rung once more, without AI, and "explain differently" — the same rung in other words, one call to AI per rung. Unsent work it does not send to reading (R69 spec);
- what the presentation style consists of and what a module says about it — О-23: `app` and `tutor` decide it. Voicing is in it, and the default mode is a field of the profile of its own (О-23, the additions of 2026-11-02);
- what a module may ask a learner under 18 to write about — П-20, closed by R127 spec: topics for under 18 are made-up and general, the reminder not to write names is shown by the platform itself — `app`, before each sending of work that goes to AI whole, — and to a learner under 18 `app` always shows where to turn;
- reporting a problem by the learner themselves — П-26, whose column "Blocks" has `app`: "Report a problem" is only the adult's (R123 spec), and to a child the section "Help" says whom to turn to. The recommendation: a teenager who gives consent themselves (R116 spec) reports themselves, in their own profile, about the tutor's words; decide before the beta with teenagers. Since T42 the button is seen only by a learner of the category `adult` — in E3 that is the account's adult themselves, — and a profile of another category does not have it, until E4 decides by П-26 how the adult reports at a reply of the tutor to a child (R33; section 3.10).

The mark "sequential", which stood here by О-19, was decided by R58 spec, and it is in section 2.8. The questions of the list were gone through by the summary of the detailing, T08: what `app` does while they are open, and in which epic of the code, is section 3.10. There too is the rest that the card assigns to `app` outside its list. О-7 and О-21 have since been closed by R82 spec, and `app` took them by the move to v0.6.6 (R12), and П-20 by R127 spec, and `app` took it by the move to v0.13.0 (R13). All eight items are decided, and deliberately put off is only what section 3.10 names.

### 2.1. Framework and build

- **Gist:** what the app is written in and how its build is assembled — the interface framework, the bundler, the type check, the linter, the tests, — and what this changes in the devcontainer. Decided here: the technologies of each part are decided by its repository (R11 spec), and the framework of each consumer of the UI packages by its repository (architecture 6 spec).
- **Decided** 2026-11-02 — R03: React 19, Vite 8, TypeScript 7, Biome, Vitest in Node and in Chromium, esbuild for CSS, `unplugin-icons`, npm; the icons are embedded by a script of its own instead of `unplugin-icons` — the 2026-11-10 amendment to R03. The design is section 3.1, and what will come with the code into the devcontainer and CI it names too.
- **Task:** T01.

### 2.2. What is stored on the device and how much

- **Gist:** which data `app` keeps on the device, in which browser storage, how much and until when — for each learner of a shared device, — what of it goes into the export and when it is erased. Decided here: it is an item of the card, "including the submission context", and it is the data of the app itself (architecture 3.1 spec, 5 spec; R36 spec). Retention periods as a whole are П-9, a question of the register of `spec`: here the periods on the device are decided, and what of them concerns П-9 goes «в реестр spec».
- **Decided** 2026-11-02 — R04: IndexedDB, a database per profile; a task's work with the contexts of its submissions — until the end of the attempt, and an unfinished one — 30 days without changes; an essay with feedback by the rubric — until it is erased; the owner's sign-out erases everything of the account from the device; what lies on the device the adult sees on the page "This device". The design is section 3.2: the table "what — where — how much — when it is erased — into the export", the storage, erasing and export. What went «в реестр spec», `spec` recorded at v0.6.6 (R12): the periods on the device — П-9 and privacy 1 and 4 spec; what erasing needs from the contract — epic E3 in RUN.md of `spec`, T63 and T64; essays on the server — П-8; "artifact" — concepts 15 spec. Since then the contract was recorded by R98 and R99 spec — the list of the account's profiles, the refusal "account gone" and `CheckPackages`, — the periods by R128 spec, П-9 closed, and artifacts on the server by R113 spec, П-8 closed; `app` took them by the move to v0.13.0 (R13).
- **Task:** T02.

### 2.3. Solving steps by the open view

- **Gist:** how `app` leads the learner through the solving steps of a task: how it reads the steps and the exit conditions from the open view, how it knows that a step is passed, what the learner sees and does at a step and how the steps meet the check, — without knowing the key. Decided here: it is an item of the card — `app` leads the steps by the open view, and the exit conditions of a step are events on the device (architecture 3.1 spec). What the steps need in the open view was decided by format v0 (R57 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 14, 18 and 19): here it is decided how the app leads the steps by it. What the steps will lack in the format goes «в реестр spec», to its next version (R44 spec).
- **Decided** 2026-11-02 — R05: `app` leads the steps on the device, without the key and without AI. The exit condition opens the button "Step done", and "Step done" only leads to the next step — work in format v0 is read on "Check", and "Check" is open when there is something in each answer slot. The chips the learner ticks by a tap, returns to a passed step, can always ask for a hint; the default mode is "by steps", the learner switches it on the task screen, and the default is set by the adult. The design and the path by steps of the three modules of the first slice are section 3.3. What went «в реестр spec», `spec` recorded at v0.6.6 (R12): "Step done" — by the 2026-11-02 amendment to R69 spec; what the steps need from the contract — in epic E3 of RUN.md of `spec`, T62, and `AttemptService` recorded it (R97 spec; R13); the module's own words at a step — in Д60 of RUN.md of `spec` and [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 18; the default mode — by a field of the profile of its own, not on the recommendation of `app` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05).
- **Task:** T03.

### 2.4. Voicing by the device's speech synthesis (Т-13)

- **Gist:** how `app` voices the tutor's words by the device's speech synthesis: which voices are allowed, what to do if there is no suitable voice, and where voicing is turned on. Decided here: the card starts voicing with the device's speech synthesis (architecture 3.1 spec). The voice as an AI capability is with `tutor` (architecture 3.6 spec), and the question of the voice itself is Т-13, in the register of `spec`.
- **Decided** 2026-11-02 — R07: `app` names the voice itself — `localService` is true, the name has no "Online", — and on Android and Linux voicing on the device is turned on by the adult; no voice — no voicing, with a note, and the text on the screen always stays. Whether voicing is on and the speed are in the profile's presentation style, and the voice is on the device. Voicing is in the first slice but not in the first app. The design is section 3.5: what is voiced, the choice of the voice, what `localService` knows on each platform, no voice, the setting, one synthesis for the whole app and the path of a hint on four devices. What went «в реестр spec», `spec` recorded at v0.6.6 (R12): why only local voices — Т-13 and architecture 8 spec, and whether voicing on Android and Linux is a disclosure — П-22; voicing in the presentation style — О-23 and privacy 4 spec; in the first slice — product 10 spec.
- **Task:** T04.

### 2.5. The adult's own key on a shared tablet (Т-9, О-4, П-17)

- **Gist:** how `app` keeps the adult's own key on a device at which a child learns too: where the key lies, when and with the request of which profile it goes, how it is connected, changed and deleted. Decided here: it is an item of the card, and the section for adults with the own key is part of `app` (architecture 3.1 spec). Whether `api` accepts the key and whether the admission function in `tutor` lets it through, they decide (Т-9, Т-20). Here too, together with T01 api, П-17 is decided — how a child will not get into the account owner's profile (privacy 9 spec).
- **Decided** 2026-11-02 — R08: the own key is only for the owner profile of the category `adult`, and it goes only with the requests of this profile that call AI. On the device the key lies only if the adult so decided on this device — in the database of the owner profile, — and otherwise it lives in the tab's memory while the owner profile is open. There is no encryption in the browser, and `app` says so plainly, and the damage is held by the limit and the expiry at the provider. The owner profile on an account with a child's profile is opened by the same PIN as the section for adults, at each opening, and closes by itself; the key does not go into the export. The design and the key's path step by step are section 3.6. What went «в реестр spec», `spec` recorded at v0.6.6 (R12): R84 spec closed Т-9 and П-17, and what the own key needs from the contract is epic E3 in RUN.md of `spec`: the field `own_key` on each request of the owner profile that calls AI, `own_key_provider` on the start of an attempt, and `CheckOwnKey` and `ListOwnKeyProviders` for the page "Own AI key" ([the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15; R97, R98 spec; R13). To a learner under 18 the own key is closed at all providers (R122 spec; П-4 closed). The providers' terms allow giving the key to `api` — so the author decided, not on the recommendation of `app` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R08).
- **Task:** T05.

### 2.6. The deployment configuration at run time

- **Gist:** how the build of `app` learns at run time the address of `api`, the parameters of the sign-in service and the other settings of the deployment, so that both the platform and self-hosting take one and the same signed build (architecture 3.1 spec). Decided here: it is an item of the card. Where the configuration comes from on the hosting and who writes it — together with `infra` (architecture 3.11 spec; Т-19); what of it will become a shared format goes «в реестр spec».
- **Decided** 2026-11-02 — R09. `app` reads the configuration at each start from `/deployment.json` at the root of its origin, beside the signed build but not in it: on the platform the file is written by the rollout of `infra` from the rollout list, and in self-hosting by the operator. The file holds the address of `api` and the parameters of Firebase Auth, without secrets. The check is strict, there are no defaults, and without a valid file `app` shows "This app isn't set up" and does not go to the network. The CSP is in two layers: the lower one is a `<meta>` in the signed `index.html`, the hosting's header is by a template from the same build, with exact addresses. Sign-in is by a pop-up window. The design is section 3.7: the settings, reading and checking, what the learner sees, the CSP, sign-in, one build for the platform and in self-hosting, the format version, the local run and the tests. What went «в реестр spec», `spec` recorded at v0.6.6 (R12): the file's format moves to `spec` with the first deployment — R85 spec; what the hosting must be able to do — Т-17 and Т-19; the addresses of the sign-in service — architecture 3.12 spec; CORS and versions — epic E3 in RUN.md of `spec` and R83 spec; the canvas's styles — the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R36 spec; the operator of a self-hosted copy — П-24. Since then the file's format has become the part of `spec` `deployment/`, and the CSP template and the layout of the archive have become rules that the rollout of `infra` reads (the 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-09 amendments to R85 spec); the build is rolled out by `infra` (R106 spec; Т-19 closed), the hosting is Firebase Hosting (R128 spec), the copy's texts are with its operator (R115 spec; П-24 closed). `app` took this by the move to v0.13.0 (R13), and how is section 3.7.
- **Task:** T06.

### 2.7. The interface languages (О-2) and accessibility

- **Gist:** in which languages the interface of the first slice is, how translations and the choice of language work, how the interface language goes with the module's explanation language, — and which accessibility `app` keeps to and how it is checked. It is one item of the card (architecture 3.1 spec). Accessibility here, «доступность интерфейса», is accessibility; the vocabulary distinguishes it from the opening of a skill and from access to content — when a skill opens to the learner and which content does not suit their age category (concepts 4, 15 spec).
- **Decided** 2026-11-02 — R10 and R11. The interface is in English and Russian. The learner's language is a field of their profile, which the adult sets, and the screens before the choice of the learner and the section for adults are in the device's language. The module's texts and the tutor's words stay in the module's explanation language, each block with its own `lang`. Translations are code of its own on `Intl`, by TypeScript dictionaries, without a library, and the strings do not give away the learner's gender. Accessibility is WCAG 2.2 level AA and two AAA criteria, 2.5.5 and 2.3.3; the answer by handwriting, the lines of the workbook and the sending of an answer without confirmation are declared nonconformities on the page "Accessibility". The standard is checked by CI — checks of its own, Biome and axe-core — and by a person on the devices of the slice. The design is sections 3.8 and 3.9, and the glossary is section 3.8. What went «в реестр spec», `spec` recorded, and `app` took it by the move to v0.13.0 (R13): the standard of R11 became the standard of all UI packages — R90 spec, and О-24 is solved; the second way of answering — О-25; translations — R91 spec and the table of words of architecture 6 spec; the interface language in the profile — R92 spec, and О-2 was added to; the legal texts — in English only, not on the recommendation of `app` (privacy 7 spec; the 2026-11-10 amendment to R10); the laws on accessibility — П-25; the name of the tutor's mark — without a reference to the phrase (R97 spec); the answer to a wrong PIN — `PinCheck` (R98 spec).
- **Task:** T07.

### 2.8. The lesson player

- **Gist:** how `app` leads a lesson by its open view: how the segments follow one another — explanations, worked examples and tasks, — how the demo opens by parts and hides, where the state of the demo is kept and what happens to it when the module's version has changed, and how the mark "sequential" is derived. Decided here: it is an item of the card that came with R58 spec (architecture 3.1 spec). The card puts it fourth, and here it is the last subsection (the opening of section 2).
- **Decided** 2026-11-02 — R06: `app` leads the segments on the device in the author's order — forward on "Continue", and back to the passed explanations and examples. The place in the lesson and the state of the demo of its examples belong to the lesson run on its package, by the lesson's ID and the package digest: a lesson on another package starts from the beginning, and the state of another version of the module does not reach the canvas element. On another device the lesson opens after the last task that ended. The mark "sequential" — each skill of the module in its own tier — is seen in "My subjects" and in the section for adults. The design and the learner's path through a lesson of olympiad maths are section 3.4. What went «в реестр spec», `spec` recorded at v0.6.6 (R12): what the player needs from the contract — epic E3 in RUN.md of `spec`, T64; what a chain is — the 2026-11-02 amendment to R58 spec. The player's contract was recorded by `LearningService` — lessons by runs — and `PackageService` (R99 spec), and `app` took it by the move to v0.13.0 by the second 2026-11-10 amendment to R06 (R13).
- **Task:** T11.

## 3. Design

Here moves what is decided by section 2: which parts the component consists of, its types and data, how it is linked with other repositories. Subsections 3.1–3.9 go in the order of the decisions, R03–R11, and section 3.10 is the summary of the detailing: what else the card assigns to `app`, the open questions of `spec` about its screens and what each epic of the code takes from the decisions.

### 3.1. Framework and build

Decided by R03. The app is web, for a tablet and a computer, without offline and native apps (product 10 spec), and without a server process of its own: its build is served by the hosting of `infra`, Firebase Hosting (architecture 1 spec, 3.11 spec; R128 spec). The versions of third-party packages in the table are the latest of those released no later than three days before installation, as in R12 canvas: those that T15 and T16 installed 2026-11-10 by the lock file of `npm install --before=2026-11-07T00:00:00Z`, and `axe-core`, which they did not install, as of 2026-11-02 (R14, R15). The packages of `@altnovatrail` do not fall under the pause: `@altnovatrail/spec` comes out on the day of the tag, and its version is the same as the submodule's tag (R73 spec), and the signature of them all is checked by `release-verify-npm` (R77 spec). They are installed after the third-party ones, without a date, and the dates of the whole lock file are checked against the registry (R14, R15). Licenses are by the field `license` of the packages in the npm registry, and for the VS Code extension by its repository (sources 2.1, 2.3).

| Role | Package | Version | License |
|---|---|---|---|
| framework | `react`, `react-dom`; `@types/react`, `@types/react-dom` | 19.3.1; 19.3.0 | MIT |
| bundler | `vite` — with Rolldown inside; `@vitejs/plugin-react` | 8.3.6; 6.2.1 | MIT |
| CSS minification | `esbuild` | 0.28.2 | MIT |
| `dev` exception | `lightningcss` and its builds for the platforms — Vite installs them | 1.33.0 | MPL-2.0 |
| type check | `typescript` | 7.0.2 | Apache-2.0 |
| Node types — for `vite.config.ts` and the tests in Node | `@types/node` | 24.19.2 | MIT |
| platform types | `@altnovatrail/spec` | the same as the submodule's tag: 0.16.3 (R38). `@altnovatrail/canvas` 0.3.15 has a nested copy of its own, 0.15.1, and `@altnovatrail/skill-map` 0.1.5 has 0.15.0: each names it in `dependencies`. These are only types, they put nothing into the build, and `ElementHoldsContract` and `ElementTakesViews` do not let them diverge from the copy of `app`: `types/canvas` and `types/formatv0` did not change between v0.15.0 and v0.16.3 of `spec` (R73 spec; R15, the amendment and the second amendment of 2026-11-11 and the third, fourth and fifth amendments of 2026-11-12 to it; R34) | Apache-2.0 |
| format and lint | `@biomejs/biome`; the VS Code extension `biomejs.biome` | 2.6.6; 3.8.1 | MIT OR Apache-2.0 |
| tests | `vitest`, `@vitest/browser-playwright`, `@vitest/coverage-v8` | 5.1.0 | MIT |
| test browsers | `playwright`; Chrome Headless Shell 1254, WebKit 2388 and Firefox 1572 from the image `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.65.0-noble` by digest | 1.65.0 — the version of the template's image whatever the date, and Dependabot keeps it in `ignore` (R14); since v0.16.3 of `spec` — the 2026-11-11 amendment to R79 spec (R38); WebKit and Firefox since v0.17.1 — the 2026-11-13 amendment to R79 spec (R43) | Apache-2.0; the browsers — named exceptions by R12 spec: Chrome Headless Shell on Google's free terms, WebKit and Firefox by the 2026-11-13 amendment to R12 spec |
| screen tests | `@testing-library/react`, `@testing-library/dom`; input — `userEvent` from `vitest/browser` | 16.3.3; 10.4.2 | MIT |
| reading the YAML of the examples and vectors of `spec` in tests | `yaml` | 2.9.1 — of 2026-09-11, without dependencies (T17) | ISC |
| accessibility check — section 3.9 | `axe-core` | 4.14.0 | MPL-2.0 — a `dev` exception (R11) |
| icons | the script `scripts/icons.ts` on `@iconify/utils`; the set `@iconify-json/material-symbols` (R78 spec; the 2026-11-10 amendment to R03) | 3.1.7; the set — the same version as that of `canvas` and `skill-map` in the lock file: 1.2.98 for `canvas` 0.3.15, as for `skill-map` 0.1.5 (the third 2026-11-12 amendment to R15) | MIT; the set — Apache-2.0 |
| contracts `app` ↔ `api` | `@altnovatrail/contracts`; `@bufbuild/protobuf`; the connect-es client — `@connectrpc/connect` and the Connect transport for the browser `@connectrpc/connect-web` (R83 spec) | `@altnovatrail/contracts` — the same as the submodule's tag: 0.16.3 (the fifth 2026-11-12 amendment to R15); `@bufbuild/protobuf` 2.17.0 — the one that the contracts package 0.16.3 names in `dependencies`; connect-es — 2.3.0 (R15) | Apache-2.0; `@bufbuild/protobuf` — `(Apache-2.0 AND BSD-3-Clause)`, and the text of BSD-3-Clause goes into the build's `NOTICE` (`npm view`; R83 spec) |
| legal texts — the Markdown body, section 3.8 | `marked`, the lexer only; the elements are built by code of its own (R27) | 18.1.3 — of 2026-11-02, without dependencies, the latest as of three days before the installation of 2026-11-11 (R14) | MIT; its `LICENSE` also holds John Gruber's Markdown license, and its text goes into the build's `NOTICE` |
| sign-in — section 3.7 | `@firebase/app`, `@firebase/auth` — those that `firebase` 13.2.0 pins, not `firebase` itself: its `firestore` pulls in `@grpc/grpc-js` 1.9.16 with a high finding (R15) | 0.16.5; 1.13.9 | Apache-2.0 |
| canvas | `@altnovatrail/canvas` | 0.3.15 — the latest as of 2026-11-12 (the third 2026-11-12 amendment to R15): since 0.3.13 — zooming the sheet up to 200 % by the buttons "Zoom in" and "Zoom out", scrolling sideways by "Left" and "Right", and the keyboard into the sheet, its region, and since 0.3.12 the row does not stick in a low window (section 3.9); before it 0.3.4 stood. Since 0.3.0 it reads format 0.4 of the submodule's tag, since 0.1.7 it sets styles through `adoptedStyleSheets` and stands under its own CSP (Д117 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`; R15) | Apache-2.0 |
| skill map | `@altnovatrail/skill-map` | 0.1.5 — the latest as of 2026-11-12, released 2026-11-11 (R34; the fourth 2026-11-12 amendment to R15): the icon set 1.2.98 — the same as the canvas 0.3.15 has, and `@altnovatrail/spec` — 0.15.0, by a copy of its own. The first release 0.1.0 came out 2026-11-08 (Д115 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). It sets the styles of its Shadow DOM through `adoptedStyleSheets` and stands under the CSP of `app` — a test of the built page holds this | Apache-2.0 |

**TypeScript.**
- `lib` — ES2022, DOM and DOM.Iterable: the types of the canvas element take the DOM types (R74 spec). `target` — ES2022, as in `canvas`; to revisit when the browsers of the devices become known.
- The strict flags are the same as in `tsconfig.base.json` of `canvas`. Its own are `moduleResolution: bundler` and `jsx: react-jsx`: modules are resolved by Vite, not by `tsc` as in `canvas` with its `NodeNext`. The package `@altnovatrail/spec` is checked under `bundler` too (R73 spec).
- `types` — by an explicit list: in TypeScript 7 it is empty by default. For the app it holds `vite/client`. `vite.config.ts` and the tests in Node have a `tsc` program of their own with `types: ["node"]`, like `tsconfig.node.json` of `canvas`.
- The canvas element in JSX — by the augmentation `declare module "react" { namespace JSX { interface IntrinsicElements { "novatrail-canvas": … } } }` by the types of `@altnovatrail/spec/canvas`, in the file `src/canvas/jsx.ts`, not `.d.ts`: in it the type `ElementHoldsContract` checks the class of the canvas element against the contract of the copy of `app` both ways, and errors in `.d.ts` are skipped by `skipLibCheck`. A trial test in Chromium holds the augmentation on the real element (R15). The types are imported by `import type`; `HTMLElementTagNameMap` and the event map are augmented by the package `canvas` itself (R74 spec). The function `media` `app` gives the element by the type `Media` of the same package (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R74 spec), and `undo()` and `clear()` are outside the contract, and `app` does not call them: "Undo" and "Clear" are buttons of the element itself (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R36 spec).
- The skill map element in JSX — the same way, in the file `src/skills/jsx.ts`: `"novatrail-skill-map"` with the types of the class `NovatrailSkillMap` and `SkillMapView` of the package `@altnovatrail/skill-map`, and the data types — `SkillGraph` and `Manifest` from `@altnovatrail/spec/formatv0/open` of the copy of `app` and `GetSkillMapResponse` from `@altnovatrail/contracts`. The type `ElementTakesViews` checks the class's properties — `graph`, `tiers`, `manifest` and `skillMap` — against these types both ways: in the element they come from its nested copy of `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.15.0, and a diverged type is an error of `ci-lint` (R73 spec; R34). `lang` is required, `view` is `learner` or `adult`, `heading-level` is from 2 to 6, the event is `onskillchoose`, in lower case; a trial test in Chromium holds the augmentation on the real element. `src/main.tsx` imports the entry point of `@altnovatrail/skill-map` before the first render, on the second line, after the canvas.
- `tsc --noEmit` only checks, and Vite builds. There are two programs: `tsconfig.app.json` — `src/` with DOM, and `tsconfig.node.json` — the configuration, `scripts/` and the tests in Node; the shared flags are `tsconfig.base.json`, with `allowImportingTsExtensions`: imports go with the extension `.ts`.

**Routes** — in the hash of the address, by code of its own (R14): `/#/<screen>/<part>/…`, the parts percent-encoded. Parsing and building the hash is `src/app/hash.ts`, without DOM, and navigation and the current route for React are `src/app/router.ts`, on `hashchange` and `useSyncExternalStore`; a hash that the build does not know is the home screen. The hosting needs no rules: for every address it serves the one `index.html` at the root. The fragment after `#` the browser sends neither to the hosting nor to `api`, and the address holds only the ID of content, a lesson or a task, and never the ID of a profile, an account or an attempt.

**React and the canvas element** — as `canvas` recorded (R42 canvas; architecture 3.3 canvas):
- the entry point of `@altnovatrail/canvas` the app imports before the first render: before the element is registered, React writes an attribute, not a property. Since T24 — `src/main.tsx`, on the first line (R20);
- React assigns properties in the order of the JSX: `task` and `history` before `marks`, `workedExample` before `demoState` (R36 spec);
- `tool` and `demoState` the app either does not give at all or gives exactly what `toolchange` and `demochange` said: a new literal on each re-render would return the demo to the beginning;
- events — in lower case, as the element sends them: `onhistorychange`, `ontoolchange`, `ondemochange`. The contract's event map is only these three; `speak` will come with the text canvas (R36 spec, R74 spec);
- a new element for each segment of a lesson — by a new `key` (section 3.4);
- `null` is not written into properties; the attribute `lang` always stands on the element itself — the screen's language, and `lang` in `CanvasProps` is required: without the attribute the canvas names the sheet's region in the page's language, and that is its nonconformity with 3.1.2 (the 2026-11-12 amendment to R11; section 3.9);
- the sheet's zoom and the pen mode are one for the whole page and hold until a reload, and `app` does nothing for them; `app` gives the element no height, and in a low window the row of buttons scrolls away with the page (architecture 3.3 canvas; the 2026-11-12 amendment to R11);
- the writing history and the submission context the app stores through JSON, as they are (section 3.2), and in the CSP it allows `img-src blob:`.

**Vite.**
- `build.cssMinify: 'esbuild'`: `lightningcss` processes nothing of what ships (R03).
- `build.outDir` — a folder of its own, not `dist/`: `dist/` holds only the release files, and in it the shared `release.yml` of `.github` accepts plain files alone — "a release takes files alone" (sources 2.1). So too in `canvas`: its build is in `lib/`, and its T66 puts the release files into `dist/`.
- The deployment configuration is not embedded: neither `define` nor variables `VITE_*`. Vite replaces them at build time, and `app` reads the configuration at run time from `/deployment.json` — section 3.7 (R09).
- `build.assetsInlineLimit: 0`: Vite does not inline small files as `data:` addresses, and the CSP does not allow them (section 3.7).
- A Vite plugin of its own: at build time it puts the lower layer of the CSP into `<head>` right after `<meta charset>` and puts `deployment.schema.json` and `csp.json` into the build. If anything else turns up before the lower layer, the build stops. In development and in `vite preview` it serves `/deployment.json` from `deployment.local.json`, and on the development server there is no lower layer: the React Refresh preamble is an inline script. The paths of `api` are led to the local `api` by `server.proxy` and `preview.proxy`, and both servers have one port, with `strictPort: true` (section 3.7).
- The development server is on `127.0.0.1`, with its own `server.fs.allow` — `index.html`, `src/` and `node_modules/`: without the list Vite would serve the whole repository (R20 canvas).
- `appType: "mpa"`: neither the development server nor `vite preview` serves `index.html` for a path that does not exist — like the hosting without rules of its own, — and `/deployment.json` without the file answers 404, not a page (R14; section 3.7).

**Packages and licenses.**
- npm from Node 24.21.0 of the stage `toolchain`. In `.npmrc` — `save-exact=true` and `ignore-scripts=true`, the lock file is of version 3.
- In `dependencies` is everything that the bundler embeds into the build: `react`, `react-dom`, `@altnovatrail/canvas`, `@altnovatrail/skill-map`, `@altnovatrail/contracts` with `@bufbuild/protobuf` and the Connect client, `@firebase/app` and `@firebase/auth` and the package of the icon set `@iconify-json/material-symbols` — so says the 2026-11-02 amendment to R39 spec, which closed Т-36. Everything else is in `devDependencies`, and `@altnovatrail/spec` too (R73 spec). On this split depend both `ci-vuln` — `npm audit --omit=dev` looks only at what ships, — and the exception of `lightningcss`: it holds only while the package is `dev` (R39 spec).
- `NOTICE` — the notice of `app` and a line `<name> <version> — <license> — <copyright>` for each package of the lock file without the flag `dev`, transitive ones included, — the license as an SPDX expression, — a line `Text of <license>` with the text of each identifier of their licenses, and for a package with a `NOTICE` file of its own, its text as it is, as a section `Notice of <name> <version>:` (Apache License 2.0, clause 4(d)); `scripts/notice.ts` in `ci-licenses` checks this against the lock file and `node_modules` both ways (R14, R15). Now these are 24 packages — since T43 also `@altnovatrail/skill-map` 0.1.5 with its copy of `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.15.0, — and a `NOTICE` of their own is in `@altnovatrail/canvas` and `@altnovatrail/skill-map`.
- Since T40 the build itself shows `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` too — the page of notices about others' code `#/legal/notices` (R31). `src/legal/notices.ts` takes their copy from the repository's files as text at build time, by Vite's `?raw` import, and a test in Node checks it against the files byte for byte; so the page loads nothing at run time and shows the same bytes as the build's archive beside `index.html`. So `server.fs.allow` of the development server names these two files too.
- The licenses of the lock file are checked by the recipe of `spec` `licenses-check-repo` by the list `licenses-allowed`, with the exceptions of `licenses.json`. The exception of `lightningcss` is only for packages with `dev`, with a reason (R39 spec), and npm is not written into this file: it is named in `spec` (the 2026-10-22 amendment to R12 spec). So too `axe-core` under MPL-2.0: it checks accessibility in tests, does not ship and puts none of its own code into what ships (R11).
- `@altnovatrail/spec` — in `devDependencies`, by a copy of its own, not `peerDependencies`. The version is raised by the move to a tag together with the submodule, and Dependabot `npm` skips it (R73 spec; Д49 and Д50 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). So too `@altnovatrail/contracts`: its version is the same as the tag, and the same move raises it, and Dependabot `npm` keeps it in `ignore` (R83 spec; the fourth 2026-11-02 amendment to R83 spec; the 2026-11-02 amendment to R21 spec; Д92 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). The contracts' code `app` imports by the path of the code file, as the package exports it — `@altnovatrail/contracts/novatrail/api/v1/attempt_pb.js`, — not by a copy and not from the submodule (Д92 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`).
- `canvas` and `skill-map` — by exact version from npm, from the scope `@altnovatrail` (R37 spec). The signature of their version and of the versions of `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` is checked by `release-verify-npm` in CI of the pull request that changes the lock file, and from then on the version is held by `integrity` (R77 spec; RULES.md, Hard rules). The first release of `@altnovatrail/canvas` — T70 in RUN.md of `canvas` — came out 2026-11-02, and as of 2026-11-10 npm had 0.1.0 to 0.3.4 (`npm view`); `app` took 0.3.4 (R15), and on 2026-11-12, at T36, 0.3.15, the latest released (the third 2026-11-12 amendment to R15). The styles of its Shadow DOM it sets since 0.1.7 through `adoptedStyleSheets`, by the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R36 spec, and it reads format 0.3 since 0.2.0 (Д117 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). The first release of `@altnovatrail/skill-map`, 0.1.0, came out 2026-11-08 (Д115 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`); `app` took on 2026-11-12, at T43, 0.1.5, the latest released, and `release-verify-npm` accepted it and its copy of `@altnovatrail/spec` 0.15.0 (R34).
- A working copy of `canvas` `app` connects in development by the recipe `just dev-canvas <folder>`, and it does not get into `main` (R15): only on the development server, by the variable `NOVATRAIL_CANVAS`, `vite.config.ts` points the entry points that the copy's `package.json` exports to its `lib/` and lets the server into its folder. `package.json` and the lock file do not change, and `npm link` is rejected: it edits them, and `release-verify-npm` names a link to a folder and skips it (R77 spec). There are no links in the lock file — `scripts/pins.ts` holds this.

**Tests** — Vitest, in four projects:
- logic — in Node;
- screens with the real canvas element — in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, an instance of each project per engine, named `<project> (<engine>)`, by the provider `@vitest/browser-playwright`, with `headless: true` explicitly (R45): in the development container there is no `CI`. Testing Library for React renders and queries the screen, and input is driven by `userEvent` from `vitest/browser`: it goes through the Chrome DevTools Protocol and does not fake events, as `@testing-library/user-event` does (sources 2.1).
- the coverage — of Node and Chromium alone: Vitest's v8 coverage refuses any other engine (sources 2.5), so `ci-test` runs them with it and then WebKit and Firefox without it (R45);
- Accessibility — section 3.9: two more projects, `forced colors` in Chromium and Firefox — WebKit has no such mode — and `reduced motion` in all three, each with its own file and with the mode of the browser context from the very start — the provider's `contextOptions`, `forcedColors: "active"` and `reducedMotion: "reduce"`, as in `canvas` (R17), — and `axe-core` on each screen, except the subtree of the canvas element; the contrast of the theme's colors — in Node.
- Coverage — `@vitest/coverage-v8`, as one report `coverage/lcov.info`, as the TypeScript wrapper of `.github` expects; the merge waits for `codecov/patch` — at least 90 % of the changed lines (R48 spec; RULES.md, How we work).
- The browser — Chrome Headless Shell from the Playwright image by tag and digest, in the stage `toolchain`, as R13 canvas; the path to it is `ENV PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`. This is a build of Google Chrome for Testing on Google's free terms — a named exception by the 2026-10-22 amendment to R12 spec, and R79 spec names it: the stage with the browser is carried by the kind `typescript` of the template, and `app` took it by the recipe `editDevcontainer` at the move to v0.6.6 (R12). Since v0.17.1 the stage also carries WebKit and Firefox from the same image, a `COPY` each, with the libraries of the three engines and the fonts of Playwright's list `tools` from one snapshot of the Ubuntu archive; they are named exceptions by the 2026-11-13 amendment to R12 spec, and the default font is WenQuanYi Zen Hei, in Chromium too (T141 `spec`; the 2026-11-13 amendment to R79 spec; R43).

**Icons** — the set Material Symbols, `@iconify-json/material-symbols`, in the style Rounded: outlined, and the selected one filled (R78 spec). They are embedded by a script of its own, as in `canvas`: `scripts/icons.ts` on `@iconify/utils` writes `src/app/icons.ts` — the viewBox and paths of only those icons that the app takes, from the set's package at the exact version, — and `ci-lint` fails if the module is not the one that the script writes; `just icons` writes it anew. They are drawn by the component `src/app/Icon.tsx`, by SVG elements, without inserting markup as a string. `unplugin-icons` with `compiler: 'jsx'` is rejected: its `@svgr/core` pulls in through Babel `caniuse-lite` under CC-BY-4.0, which is not in the list of `spec` (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R03). There are no Iconify components that load icons from the Iconify API at run time — `@iconify/react`, `iconify-icon`: a request from the device would give the learner's IP address to a third party, and the CSP does not name the hosts of Iconify.
- Which icon goes with which value, the table of architecture 6 spec says; the buttons of the canvas's row are seen in `app` on the task screen. The icon of a value that only `app` has, `app` chooses not from those in the table, and records it here (R78 spec): "Continue" of the lesson player — `arrow-circle-right`, without fill, mirrored right to left (the third 2026-11-02 amendment to R78 spec; the 2026-11-10 amendment to R06; section 3.4); "Read aloud" — `volume-up`, without fill, as is right to left: a speaker is not a direction (R36; section 3.5).
- An icon's SVG — with `aria-hidden="true"`, without `<title>` and in the color `currentColor`, and the button's caption is visible beside the icon and remains its accessible name (R78 spec; section 3.9).
- The set's version is the same as that of the versions of `canvas` and `skill-map` in the lock file of `app` (R78 spec). `scripts/pins.ts` checks it, by a test in Node, in `ci-test`: it reads the `package.json` of the installed `@altnovatrail/canvas` and `@altnovatrail/skill-map` — their `devDependencies` name the set's version, and the npm registry serves them, — and compares it with the set's version in the `dependencies` of `app`. A divergence fails the test, so a Dependabot pull request with a new canvas whose set moved is red until the set is raised in that same pull request; the set itself Dependabot keeps in `ignore`, and a package without the set in `devDependencies` the test skips. The same test holds `@bufbuild/protobuf` at the version from the `dependencies` of `@altnovatrail/contracts` and the `@firebase` packages at one copy each in the lock file (R15). So the move to v0.6.6 decided — Д61 of RUN.md of `spec` tells `app` to decide this by it (R12).
- The set's notice — Material Symbols, Google, Apache-2.0, the address `google/material-design-icons` and the version of the set's package — goes into the build's `NOTICE` as its line, together with the notices of everything embedded in it (R78 spec).

**Recipes** — by the contract of the TypeScript wrapper of `.github`, which calls them in the stage `toolchain`:
- `ci-lint` — `biome ci`, `tsc --noEmit` with both programs, `scripts/versions.ts` — only exact versions in `package.json` — and `scripts/icons.ts --check`;
- `ci-test` — `build`, then `vitest run --coverage`: the shared `typescript.yml` does not call `build`, and a build that failed fails "TypeScript / test" (R14);
- `ci-vuln` — `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high`;
- `ci-licenses` — after `npm ci`: `just -f spec/justfile licenses-check-repo package-lock.json licenses.json`, `just -f spec/justfile release-verify-npm package-lock.json`, `just -f spec/justfile release-verify spec <tag> parts/deployment-1.tar.gz` — the release file of the part `deployment/` (section 3.7) — and `scripts/notice.ts`, which reads the `NOTICE` of the packages from `node_modules`;
- `build` — `vite build`, into the folder `build/`; for manual use — also `dev`, `dev-canvas <folder>`, `preview`, `icons` and `fmt`;
- `release-files <version>` — the archive of this build, `app_<version>.tar.gz`, as the only file in `dist/` (R25): at the root, the build and beside it `NOTICE` and `LICENSE`, because the bundle does not carry the packages' license comments. `scripts/release.ts` packs it by code of its own — ustar, by name, without time and owner, with the mode `0644` for files and `0755` for folders, `gzip` without name, time and system, — and one build gives the same bytes on any machine, and `ci-test` checks the build's archive against the archive of a second build of the same tree; `deployment.json` is not in the archive, and the recipe checks this (section 3.7). It is signed by the shared `release.yml` of `.github` without the input `npm-package`, and `infra` rolls out the build only with a verified signature (R77 spec; R106 spec). Releases are immutable, and the ruleset `v-tags` forbids the tags `v*` `update`, `deletion` and `non_fast_forward`, without bypass, — the author's step Д54 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`, by the GitHub API 2026-11-12.
- `site-check <origin> <version>` — by hand, after a rollout: the archive of the release and its signature from the release's public address, accepted by `just -f spec/justfile release-verify app <version>`, then `scripts/site.ts` checks the site at `<origin>` against it, section 3.7, "Tests" (R42).

**What will come with the code.** How the devcontainer differs from the template is recorded by a decision at the same time (repositories 1 spec), and the CI files are written by the session of this repository, by the texts of architecture 3.3 .github (the 2026-10-21 amendment to R30 spec):
- the stage `toolchain` with Chrome Headless Shell and its system libraries from a snapshot of the Ubuntu archive, and the extension `biomejs.biome` with Biome as the default TypeScript formatter, are already here: the recipe `editDevcontainer` of v0.6.6 brought them (R79 spec; R12). The image was built 2026-11-02: buf, Chrome Headless Shell 1248 and Biome are in place. The move to v0.13.0 changed only the base image — `mcr.microsoft.com/devcontainers/base` 3.0.12-noble (R13), — and the image with it is not built yet;
- the tag and digest of the Playwright image and the browser revision in it are those that `playwright` from the lock file expects. `spec` raises them in the template's `kinds/typescript.Dockerfile`, and `app` takes them by its next move to a tag; if `app` needs a new `playwright` sooner, it raises all three in its own Dockerfile and writes a row «в реестр spec» (R79 spec). Otherwise `ci-test` will not find the browser;
- CI — the TypeScript job by the shared `typescript.yml` of `.github` with the permission `id-token: write`; CodeQL with the language `javascript-typescript`; Dependabot `npm` with the groups `vitest` — `@vitest/*`, `vite` and `@vitejs/plugin-react`, — `connect` and `firebase`, without major versions of `@types/node` and without `@altnovatrail/spec`, `@altnovatrail/contracts`, `@bufbuild/protobuf`, the icon set and `playwright`, and `github-actions` with the group `shared-workflows`; `.github/codecov.yml`; the check `release-verify-npm` — came with T15, on `.github` v0.1.22, and the groups `connect` and `firebase` and the `ignore` of `@bufbuild/protobuf` and of the set with T16 (R14, R15). `release.yml` with the list of `paths` — with T29 (R25): `.github/workflows/release.yml` by the shared `release.yml` of `.github` v0.1.22, `RELEASE_LINE` 0.1, on `workflow_run` of CI, `pull_request` and the button. Since T31 — `publish: ${{ github.event_name != 'pull_request' }}`: after green CI on `main` and on the button the run publishes the release, and on a pull request it is a trial that builds, signs and checks, publishing nothing (the 2026-11-12 amendment to R25).

### 3.2. Storage on the device

Decided by R04. Here: what `app` keeps on the device, where, how much and until when, what of it goes into the export, and how the learners of a shared device are kept apart. We collect only what the platform cannot work without (privacy 1 spec). There is no offline mode in the first slice (product 10 spec; Т-10), and no reading of the work on the device either (R66 spec): the device holds only what an attempt, a lesson and the export need, and a cache, so as not to ask `api` for the same thing twice.

**Storage** — IndexedDB, by its own code behind a narrow interface, without a library:
- the profile database — each learner on the device has their own: attempts with the writing history and the submissions, essays, and the state of lessons — the open view, the place and the state of the demo (section 3.4). `app` shows and writes only the selected learner's database, and opens the databases of other profiles only for housekeeping — the expiry, erasure and the page "This device". Deleting a profile is `deleteDatabase` of its database;
- the shared cache — the open views of the module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps, by the package digest of the attempt (R62 spec). This is modules' content, not the learner's data: `api` gives them without a token too (R03 api);
- the device registry — the ID of the account that signed in, and the list of profile databases. By it `app` finds the databases that are due to be erased. Since R07 it also holds the device's voices: the voice an adult chose for each language, and the permission for voicing on Android and Linux (section 3.5); since R09, Firebase's `projectId` and `apiKey` that the sign-in went with (section 3.7); and since R11, the period after which the owner profile closes by itself (section 3.6);
- the device settings — a small database of its own with the device's language that an adult chose (R10; section 3.8). This is neither the learner's data nor the account's: a sign-out and a sign-in of another account do not erase it, while "Erase everything on this device" and the browser itself do. The registry does not suit it — a sign-out erases the registry, and the sign-in screen is seen before there is an account;
- the sign-in session is held by Firebase Auth in its own IndexedDB database: `app` sets it up through `initializeAuth` with `indexedDBLocalPersistence` alone, "LOCAL using indexedDB", not through `getAuth`, whose default storage the documentation names only as "platform-specific default dependencies". The session lives until a sign-out — "An explicit sign out is needed to clear that state" (sources 3.1) — and Firebase Auth's `signOut()` erases it;
- the media files of the device part `app` takes from `api` by a `fetch` request and gives to the canvas as a `Blob` — by the function `media` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R04; section 3.7); and `LICENSE` and `NOTICE`, since T41, it takes by the same `fetch` as text and shows on its own page of the module's license, as they are, rather than opening them by a link: a link would lead to another origin, the address of `api`, and `app` has no external links (privacy 7 spec; R32). The answers lie in the browser's HTTP cache: `fetch` goes through it, as `<img>` does (R03 api; sources 3.1, 7.1). `app` neither keeps nor erases them: the browser runs the HTTP cache, and `api` no longer serves the files of a withdrawn version (R63 spec);
- `app` puts none of its own data in localStorage and sessionStorage: they hold 5 MiB per origin, they are synchronous, and Safari's seven days erase them just as they erase IndexedDB (sources 3.1). What Firebase Auth itself writes in them at a sign-in through Google its documentation does not say, so a sign-out clears them too — all of localStorage and sessionStorage of `app`'s origin;
- there is no PIN on the device — `api` holds its hash (R04 api). `app` gets the account's list of profiles from `api` after the sign-in and holds it in memory, and the selected learner is only in memory too: after a reload `app` starts again with the choice of the learner. The owner profile on an account with a child's profile is opened by the same PIN as the section for adults, at each opening, and it also closes by itself (R08; section 3.6).

Each connection of `app` to IndexedDB closes on the event `versionchange`: otherwise `deleteDatabase` from this or another tab would wait for it, and "The blocked event fires if other clients still hold a connection to the database after their versionchange events have fired" (sources 3.1). `app` says "Erased" only on the success of `deleteDatabase`, and what it failed to erase — the database stayed in the registry — it erases again at the next launch, before it opens anything.

The names of the databases and the stores and the schema version were decided by R16 in T18 — below, "Code". The storage logic is behind the interface: the tests in Node put in a storage in memory, and the real IndexedDB is checked by the browser project, in the three engines (section 3.1).

**How the work is written.**
- `app` appends the events `historychange` to the profile database by the key "attempt, event number". A storage whose `from` is not equal to the number of events written has missed events and reads `history` again (architecture 3.3 canvas).
- With each submission the canvas gives its context: on a handwriting sheet, where the crop lies on the sheet, its integers, the group boxes of the stroke groups, the table "group label → stroke IDs" and the submission's digest, and on a workbook, the table "box → stroke IDs" and the digest. A workbook's work is `{ cells, submission }` from `exportWork()`. By the digest the canvas accepts the context back, together with the marks of that submission (architecture 3.1 spec; R36 spec). The group boxes stay on the device until Т-31 decides whether they go with the submission.
- `app` keeps the history and the context as the canvas gave them, without transformations. IndexedDB stores a record by value, by structured cloning (sources 3.1), and, like JSON, does not round numbers, and the element would not accept back a context with rounded numbers (R36 spec; R27 canvas). A test in the browser checks that the history and the context pass through the element after a write and a read: the sheet opens as it was, and the marks with that context are laid on it.
- For each submission `app` keeps its context, the length of the history at the moment of the submission, and the work for checking itself — the PNG of the crop or the crops of the boxes. The context is needed while the marks of that submission may come (architecture 3.3 canvas), and the work for going over the course of the solution: the server does not store the picture, and the app sends it once more (architecture 4.1 spec).
- Since T38 the pictures of the work lie as bytes — an `ArrayBuffer` with its type beside it — not as a `Blob`: WebKit in an ephemeral session does not write a `Blob` (В51, option (a); the 2026-11-12 amendment to R16; measurements 2). On reading it is a `Blob` again. A `Blob` that schema version 1 put there earlier is read as it is, without migration; a record whose work the build cannot read gives "no work" and stays until the end of the attempt, the expiry or an erasure. The submissions are read by a transaction of their own, after the attempt and the writing history, and a record the browser will not give does not take them down with it; the work of a correct submission, too, is read before the erasing transaction, not in it.
- The cache is written with `durability: "relaxed"`, as IndexedDB advises for caches. The work — with the browser's default setting, without `"strict"`: that one waits for the write to disk at each stroke, and IndexedDB advises it only where the risk of loss matters more than the device's performance and battery (sources 3.1). A power failure right after a stroke may cost the last strokes — the learner sees them and will write them again.

**The end of an attempt, and the essay.**
- An attempt has ended when `api` has said that the task has ended — a correct answer, the showing of the task's solution, the attempt closed "unchecked" on a withdrawn version (R63 spec), or left unfinished: the learner left it or abandoned it when it had wrong answers (R82 spec) — or when the learner has begun the same task anew. An attempt without wrong answers that the learner left gives no evidence (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R80 spec), and on the device its work lies, like that of any unfinished one, for 30 days. If `api` closed the attempt itself, `app` learns of it by the contract and erases it, like any that has ended: the profile's open attempt and open runs it reads in `GetAccount` of `AccountService` (R98, R99 spec), and the end of an attempt comes in the answer to a submission, `Done` or `ShowSolution` of `AttemptService` (R97 spec).
- An essay is the work of a task whose answer slot is bound `sheet`: "the whole sheet is the answer: a work as a whole, such as an essay, which a rubric gives feedback on". The check is closed in the open view together with its kind, while the binding is open, and in format v0 `sheet` takes only `rubric` ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 14 and 15). So `app` knows an essay even before the check. With an essay are kept what was read and the feedback by the rubric: the server stores neither the essay's text, nor the justifications, nor the words of the feedback — only how each submission ended and the marks of the criteria (R87 spec; П-19 closed).

**What lies on the device** — for each learner of a shared device. "How much" is an order of magnitude: the size of the writing history is estimated and, since T25, measured with a mouse in Chromium (measurements 1), but not with a pen or a finger (Д4 in table 4 of RUN.md). Beyond the column "When it is erased", everything in the profile database is also erased by the deletion of the profile, and everything by the owner's sign-out and the deletion of the account; the events in order are below.

| What | Where | How much | When it is erased | In the export |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the attempt's writing history, but for an essay | profile database | from hundreds of KB to a few MB per attempt; the measurement with a mouse — measurements 1 | the end of the attempt; an unfinished one — after 30 days without changes | yes, of an unfinished attempt |
| the attempt's submissions: the context, the length of the history at the moment of the submission, the work for checking | profile database | the context — a few KB, the work — tens of KB | together with the attempt; the work of a correct submission — when the correct answer words have come or their request has ended, and if `app` was closed earlier, at the next launch | the context — yes; the work — no: the history derives it |
| the attempt's step state: the attempt's mode, the step's place, the ticks of the chips, the IDs of the techniques given (R05; section 3.3) | profile database | bytes | the end of the attempt, for an essay too; for an unfinished one — together with its work: after 30 days without changes, and for an essay — when an adult erases it | yes, of an unfinished attempt |
| the open view of the task or the instance | profile database | a few KB | together with the attempt, for an essay — together with the essay; a withdrawn version — at once, for an essay too | no: it is the module's content |
| an essay — from the start of the attempt, finished or not: the writing history, what was read, the feedback by the rubric | profile database | as an attempt's history | when an adult erases it on the page "This device"; neither the end of the attempt nor the expiry erases it | yes |
| the lesson's state — by the lesson's full ID and the package digest, with the run's ID beside them: the lesson's open view, the place — the segment the learner has reached — and the state of the demo of its examples (R06; section 3.4) | profile database | a few KB | the lesson ended — passed, "unchecked", "not suitable" or "abandoned"; 30 days without changes; the withdrawal of the version; the same lesson on another package; another run under the same key | the place and the state of the demo — yes; the open view — no |
| the open views of the manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps | shared cache, by the package digest | tens of KB per package | 30 days without access; a withdrawn version — at once | no |
| media files, `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` | the browser's HTTP cache | by the package | by the browser's rules | no |
| the sign-in session | the Firebase Auth database | a few KB | the sign-out; a revoked sign-in | no |
| the heartbeat records of `@firebase/app` — the SDK's versions and the days when the SDK ran, up to 30 (section 3.7) | its own database `firebase-heartbeat-database` | bytes | when the SDK has sent them with a request to Firebase Auth; "Erase everything on this device" and the browser itself | no: it is not the learner's data |
| the device registry | a database of its own | bytes | the sign-out | no |
| the device's voices — the voice an adult chose for each language, and the permission for voicing on Android and Linux (R07; section 3.5) | the device registry, the key `voicing` (R36) | bytes | the sign-out; a sign-in of another account | no: it is not the learner's data |
| the period for closing the owner profile — 5, 15, 30 or 60 minutes of a hidden page (R11; the 2026-11-10 amendment to R08; section 3.6) | the device registry | bytes | the sign-out | no: it is not the learner's data |
| the device's language that an adult chose (R10; section 3.8) | the device settings | bytes | "Erase everything on this device"; the sign-out and a sign-in of another account — no | no: it is not the learner's data |
| the own key — the provider, the key and its last four characters (R08; section 3.6); the first app does not have it | the owner profile's database, only with the mark "Keep on this device"; without it — the tab's memory, while the owner profile is open | bytes | "Remove key" — on the page "Own AI key" or "This device" —, a replacement and the mark taken off; without the mark — the closing of the owner profile | no: it is the owner's secret, not the learner's data |

The state of the demo of another version of the module never reaches the element: a lesson's state belongs to a run on one package, and a lesson on another package starts from the beginning (R06; section 3.4). An essay on a withdrawn version of a module stays without the open view of its task: the device keeps no open views of a withdrawn version (R63 spec), and unsuitable content may have been the reason for the withdrawal. On the page "This device" and in the export such an essay comes with a note that the module's version was withdrawn — without the task's statement.

**How much space.**
- Quotas: Chromium gives an origin up to 60% of the disk; Safari 17 and newer, about 60%; Firefox without persistent storage, the lesser of 10% of the disk and 10 GiB (sources 3.1). A quota is an estimate, not a promise: a write beyond it is `QuotaExceededError`.
