- Reduced modes: in Chrome's incognito, about 5% of the disk; with the setting "Clear cookies and site data when you close all windows", about 300 MB; and private modes erase the data at the end of the session (sources 3.1).
- The estimate of the writing history: a point is `x`, `y`, `t` and `p` (`@altnovatrail/spec/canvas`), about 40–70 bytes in a record; a stroke is tens of points, because the canvas writes no point closer than 0.1 unit to the previous one (architecture 3.1 canvas); a work is up to hundreds of strokes. That is from hundreds of KB to a few MB per attempt — orders of magnitude below the quota. `app` sets no limit of its own: the end of the attempt and the 30-day expiry hold the volume.
- The measurement with a mouse in Chromium, T25, is within this estimate: a work of hundreds of strokes is hundreds of KB, and the conclusion that no limit of its own is needed holds; with a pen and a finger the rate of points is higher, and their measurement waits for devices in E3 (measurements 1).
- If a write did not go through, `app` tells the learner that the work was not saved on the device, and the page "This device" tells the adult.

**What is erased when** — by events:
- the end of an attempt — its submissions and the step state, and for a task that is not an essay, also the writing history and the task's open view. An essay, its history and the open view of its task stay. The work of a correct submission `app` erases not at once but when the correct answer words have come or their request has ended: the work goes with that request, although "correct" has already closed the attempt. If `app` was closed earlier, the next launch erases it: a repeat of the request would not bring the words (the second 2026-11-10 amendment to R04);
- the expiry — at launch `app` goes through the databases of all the profiles in the registry and erases the unfinished attempts, but for essays, and the lessons that have not changed for 30 days, and from the shared cache the records it has not accessed for 30 days, as R42 canvas-api does. So the expiry holds for a profile that has not been selected for a long time too;
- a lesson ended — passed, "unchecked", "not suitable" or "abandoned" — its state: the open view, the place and the state of the demo. The lesson's state on the former digest is erased the same way when `api` gave the same lesson on another package (section 3.4);
- the withdrawal of a version — its open views from the shared cache and from the profile databases, and the state of the lessons on its digest, as soon as `app` has learned of the withdrawal from `api` (R63 spec): about withdrawal it asks `CheckPackages` of `PackageService` at launch and after the refusal `CAUSE_VERSION_NOT_GIVEN`, the answer `410`, and the end of a run or of an attempt "unchecked" (R99 spec; Д104 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`);
- the account owner's sign-out — everything of the account: the databases of all the profiles, the shared cache, the registry and the session. Before the sign-out `app` names what will be lost: the essays that are not on the server (П-8), and the unfinished works;
- a sign-in of another account — if the registry holds another account ID, `app` erases everything of the former one before it opens anything. The device settings are not the account's data, and it does not touch them (section 3.8). This happens when the former sign-in ended without a sign-out. A user signed in without a NovaTrail account gives no account ID: `api` answers them with the refusal `CAUSE_NO_ACCESS`, "no access" — and if the registry names an account at that time, this is a sign-in of another account too, and `app` erases what is left of the former one the same way (the comment of `AccountService` in `novatrail.api.v1`; R98 spec; the 2026-11-10 amendment to R04);
- a `projectId` or an `apiKey` in `/deployment.json` other than in the registry — the Firebase Auth database and the origin's web storage, before `initializeAuth`, while the learners' data stays until the sign-in: if another account has signed in, the rule above erases it (section 3.7);
- the deletion of a profile on this device — its database, at once;
- the deletion of a profile on another device — at launch `app` compares the profile databases with the account's list of profiles from `api` and erases the extra ones. It erases only by an answer of `GetAccount` that has come, and for the account that the device registry names: a network failure erases nothing (R98 spec);
- the deletion of the account — `api` answers that the account is gone, and `app` erases everything of the account and signs out (architecture 4.5 spec), while the device settings stay;
- the buttons of the page "This device" — a profile's data or everything, and at the owner profile also the own key; "Erase everything on this device" erases the device settings too;
- "Remove key" — on the page "Own AI key" or "This device" —, a replacement of the key and the mark "Keep on this device" taken off — the own key (section 3.6);
- the browser itself — when space is short, after seven days in Safari without the app being touched, or when the user cleared the site's data. The browser erases the origin's storage whole (sources 3.1), so after that `app` starts with the sign-in. While `app` is open, this shows by the event `close` of the IndexedDB connection. But that event also comes on corrupted data or an I/O error (sources 3.1), so `app` opens the registry anew: no registry — the data on this device was erased, and `app` says just that; the registry is there — the data could not be read, and `app` says that, erasing nothing.

**The learners of a shared device.** Each one's data is in their own database, and `app` shows only the selected learner's database. This is `app`'s own order, not a security boundary: the origin is one, and whoever holds the device can read all the databases with the browser's tools. The PIN is a barrier of the interface (R07 canvas-api; R04 api), and what cannot be undone is confirmed by a fresh sign-in (architecture 4.5 spec). The owner profile on an account with a child's profile is closed by the same PIN as the section for adults, and the own key lies on the device only if the adult so decided (section 3.6).

**The export from this device** — the column "In the export": unfinished attempts with the contexts of their submissions and the step state, essays with what was read and the feedback by the rubric, and for unfinished lessons, the place and the state of the demo; from stage 5, photos from paper. The cache, the session, the registry, the work for checking and the own key do not go into the export: these are modules' content, housekeeping data, what is derived from the writing history, or the owner's secret (R08). Nor does the export say anything of the mark "Keep on this device". Works from other devices are exported from them (architecture 4.5 spec). The format of the export is decided by the epic E4 in RUN.md of `spec`, and the first app does not need it (R09 spec, R76 spec).

**The page "This device"** — in the section for adults, behind the PIN, and in E3 without the PIN, like the whole section (R29). Since T38 it is built — `#/adults/device` (the 2026-11-12 amendment to R04) — and what is on it already and what is still to come is said below, after the list:
- for each profile — the unfinished works, the essays and the space they take; the total on the device — by `navigator.storage.estimate()`, which is an estimate (sources 3.1);
- whether the browser gave persistent storage — `navigator.storage.persisted()` — and a button to ask for it, `persist()`. An adult presses it: Firefox asks with a window, while Chrome and Safari decide by themselves, without a window — Chrome by how often the site is opened, whether it is bookmarked and whether it is installed, Safari, for example, by whether the site was opened from the Home Screen (sources 3.1). If the browser refused, the page says just that: the data is kept, but the browser may erase it;
- a warning: the browser may erase the site's data by itself when space is short, and Safari after seven days without the app being touched. The advice is to add the app to the Home Screen: its web app is exempt from the seven days. The data of the web app from the Home Screen lives apart from Safari, so one has to sign in there anew, and the unfinished works and essays from the tab will not move there (sources 3.1);
- at each essay — "Erase", and also the buttons "Erase the profile's data on this device" and "Erase everything on this device";
- voicing — the device's voices by language and the permission on Android and Linux (section 3.5);
- the device's language — the same choice as on the sign-in screen (section 3.8);
- on an account with a child's profile — after how many minutes of a hidden page the owner profile closes by itself (section 3.6);
- at the owner profile, if the key is kept on this device — the line "The own key is kept on this device" with the provider and the last four characters, and the button "Remove key" — the same as on the page "Own AI key" (section 3.6).

At the first sign-in a single line says that the learners' works are kept on this device, and calls for the same button `persist()`.

What is on the page since T38 (the 2026-11-12 amendment to R04):
- for each profile, the owner first — the number of unfinished tasks and "about" how many bytes the profile's data takes: `app`'s estimate by the records of its database — strings in UTF-8, a number 8 bytes, a picture by its own size. The browser does not name the space of one database. A profile without a database on the device — "no data"; a database that cannot be read — "can't be read", and it can be erased even then. The number of tasks is a count without reading the records, and it is visible even when the space cannot be counted. A database whose erasure waits for the browser — "not all of it was erased", and the button stays, to erase again;
- at each profile — a warning that its works are only on this device and will be lost if the site's data is erased (R113 spec). While `api` keeps no copies (Д12 `api`), it stands at every profile, whatever `work_storage` says. Since T39 this is decided so (R30): storage switched on gives no copy on the server — `api` records the consent, but the contract v0.15.1 has no request that would send a work for storage (В53 in table 3 of RUN.md) — and `copiedOnServer` in `DevicePage.tsx` is "no" at each profile until this changes (Д16);
- the estimate `estimate()` for the whole site; `persisted()` and the button `persist()`, while the browser has not agreed — and for a browser without this API neither the line nor the button; the warning about erasure by the browser and about Safari's seven days, and the advice about the Home Screen — it stands with persistent storage too: whether `persist()` saves a Safari tab from the seven days the primary source does not say (sources 3.1);
- "Erase the profile's data on this device" — its database through the registry, and what the browser did not erase, the next launch erases;
- "Erase everything on this device" — the sign-out with everything it erases, and beyond that the device settings and the heartbeat database of `@firebase/app`. Its connection is held by the SDK, which does not close it on `versionchange` (`@firebase/app` 0.16.5, `openDB` without a handler), so the deletion is `blocked` while the tab is open and will go through when the connection closes (R16). After the erasure — the sign-in screen and "home";
- both erasures ask first, and the question takes the focus and gives it back (section 3.9). The device's language is on the first page of the section (R29), not here.

Not on the page yet: "Erase" at an essay — T47; the own key at the owner profile — T46; the closing period of the owner profile — E4. The device's voices and the permission for voicing on Android and Linux are on it since T45 — as the part "Voicing" after the profiles (section 3.5, "Code"). Their places are marked in `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx`.

**Code** — since T18, 2026-11-10 (R16):
- the databases: `novatrail-settings` — the device's language; `novatrail-registry` — the account, the sign-in project and the account's databases; `novatrail-cache` — the open views by `[digest, view]`; `novatrail-profile-<profile ID>` — the attempts, the writing history by `[attempt ID, n]`, the submissions, the work of a correct submission until the correct answer words, and the lessons by lesson ID. All are of version 1, and the table of the stores, keys and indexes is in R16;
- the interface — `src/storage/backend.ts`: the operations of IndexedDB that `app` takes. Its implementations are `src/storage/idb.ts` on the browser's IndexedDB and `src/storage/memory.ts`, a storage in memory for the tests in Node, with IndexedDB's order of keys, the cloning of records and the rollback of a failed transaction; `src/storage/device.ts` holds one connection per database and says that a connection was taken away or that a database is newer than the build;
- the rules — `src/storage/registry.ts`, `profile.ts`, `cache.ts`, `settings.ts`, `erase.ts` — the erasure events that go beyond one database — and `start.ts` — the launch. Each write of the work says whether it was saved, and since T24 the task screen tells the learner "Your work isn't saved on this device" (section 3.3, "Code");
- since T38 — the work as bytes and the page "This device": in `profile.ts`, `keptWork` and `workOf`, the bytes and both forms on reading, `usage` and `profileUsage` for the page; in `erase.ts`, `eraseProfile` and `eraseDevice`, and "Erase everything" is `Session.signOut(true)`; in `browser.ts`, `siteStorage`: `estimate()`, `persisted()` and `persist()`; the page is `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx`. The storage in memory can also be a store whose records the browser does not give (`unreadable`): the tests in Node check that the attempt and the history come without the submissions;
- the launch — in `src/boot/Boot.tsx`, after `/deployment.json` has been read and before the app, and the app gets the storage through `useStorage()`. What to do with a connection that the browser or another tab took away is decided by `src/boot/storage.ts`: the browser closed the connection — the screen "The data on this device was erased" or "The data on this device can't be read"; another tab opened the database at a new version — an offer to reload; erased it — a sign-out; a deleted profile — nothing (R16). The launch is one per tab, under StrictMode too;
- the open views, the cache and the files of the device part — `src/packages/` since T21 (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R16):
  - `views.ts` — a view from the shared cache, otherwise from `api`, and then into the cache. Since T23 `keptManifests` gives, in one pass and without a request, the manifests that the cache holds — per module, of the latest version — and by them "My subjects" names a module without a current version; and one and the same view asked for twice at once `api` gets in one request. A cache record is the bytes of the view's JSON, the format version and, for the skill graph, the tiers, and a part that the module does not have is a record without bytes. The build's window of versions is `0.4`: a view of another version does not go into the cache, and `app` offers to reload, and a view that the cache holds in a version outside the window is thrown away, and `app` asks `api` anew. Only a view that was read as JSON goes into the cache, and a view of a package whose withdrawal this tab has already learned of does not. A request waits 30 s by its own signal, not by a header. `CAUSE_VERSION_NOT_GIVEN` from any service calls `CheckPackages` — this is the client's event `withdrawn`; before the sign-in there is no cache, and the view is simply read;
  - `checks.ts` — `CheckPackages` about the digests whose open views lie in the shared cache and in the profile databases — without the attempts whose task view the withdrawal has already taken — at the launch of `app`, once the storage has started, and on the signs of withdrawal. What was withdrawn is erased (`versionsWithdrawn`), and about a digest that `api` does not know `app` asks again after 60 s. One check at a time, and a reason that comes during it gives one more after it;
  - `files.ts` — a file of the part at the address of `api` `/packages/sha256-<hex>/device/<path>`, each segment of the path percent-encoded, and a path with an empty segment, `.` or `..` is not a path of the part; `fetch` without headers: `200` — a `Blob`, `404` — no such path, `410` — the version was withdrawn, and `CheckPackages`, `503` — a repeat after `Retry-After`, but no later than 30 s, and without it and on a network failure, a repeat after the client's growing pauses. `media(digest)` gives the canvas the function `media`. Since T41 a body cut off on the way is a network failure too, and `text(digest, path)` gives `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` as UTF-8 text, as they are, and `404` — "no file"; `license.ts` reads both one after the other for the page of the module's license, and `410` — "the version is no longer given" and one `CheckPackages` (R32; section 3.10, "Licenses of modules");
- the sign-in and the account — `src/account/session.ts` since T22 (R19): after each sign-in the registry remembers the sign-in project, and by the answer of `GetAccount` the registry's former account and everything that the registry still holds for erasure are erased before the app opens — a database that another tab holds the app waits for with "Try again" — and the profiles that are not in the list are erased too; on "no access" the client erases (R18), and a network failure erases nothing. The sign-out is first `signOut()`, then `eraseAccount`, and if the sign-in service did not sign out, nothing is erased, and the sign-out screen names for each profile how many unfinished attempts its database has (`Profile.unfinished()`). The line at the first sign-in and the button `persist()` are on the screen of the choice of the learner, and "first" means that the registry has not named this account before;
- the erasure events are to be called by those who learn of them: T21 — the withdrawal of a version, T22 — the sign-in, the sign-out and the account, T24 — the end of an attempt, a correct answer by choice and a closed attempt (done, above and section 3.3, "Code"), T25 — the work of a correct submission until the correct answer words, T27 — the end of a lesson (done, section 3.4, "Code"). The attempt's step state is `steps` of its record, whose form `src/task/steps.ts` reads (R20);
- checked in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, in CI since T57 (R45): an opening without creation does not set up a database — an interrupted upgrade of a new database does not leave it behind; `versionchange` closes the connection, and a deletion and a new version from another connection go through; a connection that does not close by itself gives `blocked`; bytes and numbers pass a write and a read without rounding; a workbook's work goes into IndexedDB as bytes with a type and is read as a `Blob`, and a `Blob` of schema version 1 is read after the fix (T38); the writing history and the submission context pass through the canvas element after IndexedDB, and the element rejects a context with rounded numbers. The `Blob` of schema version 1 — in Chromium and Firefox only: a context of Playwright is an ephemeral session, where WebKit writes no `Blob` (measurements 2). What a device alone shows is Д13 in DEBT.md.

**Later.**
- Photos from paper — stage 5 (О-11): a store of their own in the profile database, which is erased with the profile and goes into the export, like an essay.
- The learner model lives in the database of `api`, and the engine computes on the server (R80 spec): П-7 is closed, and the device does not have it.

### 3.3. Solving steps

Decided by R05. `app` leads the solving steps itself, on the device: the steps and their exit conditions are in the open view of the solving steps, the chips "givens and unknown" in the open view of the task, and no condition needs the key (architecture 3.1 spec; R57 spec). The steps do not call AI: the work is read only on "Check", and "Step done" does not read it (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R69 spec).

**Where the steps come from.** The open view of the solving steps `api` gives by the package digest of the attempt, once per package, and it lies in the shared cache (R62 spec; section 3.2). The types are `SolvingSteps` and `Step` from `@altnovatrail/spec/formatv0/open`: a step has `title`, `gives` and `exit_condition`, and from format 0.4 also an optional `description`, how to go through the step, in the module's explanation language ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 18; R108 spec). The steps are one set for all the tasks of a module, a task cannot replace them, and in a module without steps the tasks are solved without them.

**The screen in the mode "by steps".** Beside the canvas is the tutor's panel:
- the steps bar — the titles in order, with the place of each: passed, current and not yet open. A tap on a passed step returns to it, and only "Step done" leads forward;
- the current step — its title, and beside it its `description`, if it has one — with its own `lang`, like all the module's content (section 3.8) —, what the step gives — the chips and the technique cards — and a line of the interface on what will open the button (R108 spec; Д121 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`);
- the button "Step done" — at every step but the last. The last one ends on "Check": it has no next step, and its exit condition locks nothing — `app` shows the line of that condition, and `answer_slots_filled` opens "Check". In the format examples the last step is `none`;
- "Check" and "Hint" are on the task screen always, in both modes.

The canvas is open at every step: a step does not lock the work, and the learner writes when they want. There are no timers and no automatic advance (product 7 spec).

**Exit conditions** — the lock of the button "Step done":

| Condition | When the button is open | Event on the device | `app`'s line on the step |
|---|---|---|---|
| `givens_ticked` | all the `givens` and `unknown` that the task has are ticked; for a task without them the step passes by itself | a tap on a chip in the panel | Mark what we know and what to find |
| `answer_slots_filled` | there is something in each answer slot | the canvas's `historychange` — `app` reads `filled` again; a tap on an option of `choice` | Write an answer in each slot |
| `none` | at once | — | none |

`answer_slots_filled` — by the slot binding (the second 2026-11-01 amendment to R36 spec; R42 canvas):
- `anywhere` and `sheet` — the slot is the only one in the task, and the condition is `filled[0][0]`: whether the sheet has a stroke that `exportWork()` would take. It is met with the first stroke, so it only opens the button and does not lead further by itself;
- `choice` — its own choice of an option: `filled` of a sheet with only `choice` slots is `null`;
- a workbook box — `filled[item − 1][box − 1]`, at each box of each line;
- `zone` is taken by no module, and the canvas element does not accept a task with it.

`filled` changes before `historychange`, and assigning `task` and `history` changes it without an event, so `app` reads it also after it has opened the task. `app`'s lines on the step are the interface: their language, rules and words are section 3.8, and they are written without gender and without addressing the learner (R10).

**The chips** are shown and ticked by `app` (architecture 3.9 canvas): `givens` as a list, `unknown` apart, as the module gave them. A tap ticks a chip, a second tap unticks it. There is no wrong tick: the chips retell the statement ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14). For a task with only `givens` or only `unknown` the step waits for those there are. If a step gives `givens` but has another condition, the chips are visible, but ticking them is not required.

**Technique.** When a step whose `gives` gives `techniques` begins, `app` names to `api` the attempt and the step's place. `api` answers with the IDs of the task's techniques — empty if the task has no techniques — and records which technique it gave ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 19; R57, R82 spec). A step that gives only `givens` does not go to `api`: "givens and unknown" are open, `app` shows them from the task's open view, and `api` does not record that the learner saw them (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05). A technique is closed, and `app` does not know whether the task has one. The names and descriptions `app` takes from the open technique catalog in the cache (R62 spec) and shows them as a card on the step; empty — the step shows only its title. The IDs of the techniques given `app` keeps with the attempt, and a return to the step and a relaunch do not ask `api` for them anew. In the contract this is `BeginStep` of `AttemptService`: the answer is the IDs of the task's techniques, and "Step done" sends no request (R97 spec).

**Modes.**
- The default mode is set by an adult for each profile in the section for adults, and for a new profile it is "by steps". Each task begins in it. Since T37 it is set by the section's profile page (R29; section 3.10): `app` takes it from the profile of the selected learner as `GetAccount` or the answer of `UpdateProfile` gave it — in this tab or in another, by `BroadcastChannel` — and an attempt that has no step state on this device yet begins in it. An attempt already begun keeps its own mode.
- The learner switches the mode on the task screen at any moment (product 4 spec). Their choice holds until the end of the attempt and is kept with its step state, and it does not change the default mode. The steps are kept meanwhile: back in "by steps", the learner goes on from the step where they were.
- The default mode is a field of its own in the learner's profile on the server, not part of the presentation style: so `spec` answered the row В14, not by the recommendation of `app` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05; privacy 4 spec; concepts 15 spec). It follows the learner to any device, and does not go to AI; in the contract it is a field of the profile of `AccountService` (R98 spec).
- "Self" — there is no steps panel, a technique comes only on request, and the tutor answers only when called ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 18; concepts 10 spec). On the button the mode is called «Самостоятельно»: in Russian «сам» gives away the learner's gender (section 3.8).
- A module without steps has no switch: the screen is as in the mode "self".

**"Check", the pause and the hint.**
- "Check" — in both modes and at any step. It is open when the same holds as for `answer_slots_filled`: an empty sheet and empty boxes do not go to AI. A tap on an option of `choice` only chooses, and "Check" sends: the value goes to `api` and is checked without AI (architecture 4.2 spec).
- The pause — 6 s without a stroke: "Check" blinks, if it is open, in both modes. Neither reading nor AI (R69 spec). One blink per pause — shorter than 5 s and no more than 3 times a second — and it comes again only after a new stroke or a choice of an option. With `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` there is a still highlight instead, and the blinking says nothing to a screen reader (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05; section 3.9). "Step done" does not blink on a pause: at the step "solve" with a slot `anywhere` it is open from the first stroke, and the blinking would call onward before the answer.
- "Hint" — always, in both modes, from the start of the task. It does not send the work (R69 spec). By R82 spec:
  - before the first wrong answer — "givens and unknown" from the task's open view, the technique and the task's leading question, `leading_question`, which `api` gives: the request `GetHint` goes for them, and `api` records what was given. The leading question is a closed field of the format, only this answer carries it, and empty means the task has none; `app` shows it beside the technique, with its own `lang`. The open attempt holds it in `requested_leading_question`, and when the attempt is continued — after a relaunch and on another device — `app` shows it from there. After the first wrong answer the question is no longer given (R108 spec; the 2026-11-08 amendment to R82 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14; [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15; Д121 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`);
  - after a wrong answer — the words of the current rung, which the attempt keeps, once more, without AI, and if there are none, the rung's fallback text;
  - "Explain differently" — the same rung in other words: one call of AI per rung, without the work, with the task's past questions. A second time on the same rung `api` does not give it, and in the mode without AI (О-5) there is no button.

**After the check.**
- "Not yet" — the learner stays on their step. `app` asks for the tutor's words, sending the work once more, and shows the rung's question and the marks on the tutor's layer (architecture 4.1 spec). One can go back to the plan and the technique with a tap on the steps bar.
- "Unreadable" — with a reason, and the learner writes it again (R56 spec; concepts 9.1 spec). The reasons are the format's `blank` and `notation` and four more from the observation: `illegible` — cannot be made out, `ambiguous` — reads two ways, `no_final_answer` — no final answer, and `several_final_answers` — several final answers (R86 spec; [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 10; Д81 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). The words of each are by R10, and a reason that the build does not know is a general line. An AI failure or a quota used up is a neutral phrase (R04 spec).
- From the fourth wrong answer, when the hint ladder has been passed, `app` offers a child to call an adult or to show the solution, and to a learner of the category `adult` the button "Show the solution" is visible from the start of the attempt (R82 spec). "Call a grown-up" is the interface of `app`: `api` notifies no one. If the adult's setting "ask before showing" is on for the child — by default it is on —, the showing goes as an adult action behind the PIN of the section for adults, and `api` rejects the learner's request with the reason "an adult is needed", and `app` says just that. The showing closes the attempt with the outcome "solution shown", and only then or after a correct answer does the task's solution come to the device (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05 spec).
- Whether the task has ended `api` decides: a correct answer, the showing of the task's solution, "unchecked" on a withdrawn version (R63 spec). The steps panel closes, and what the learner sees next is the screen after the check: what is on it section 3.10 names, and the epic E2 builds the layout.
- An essay — reading and feedback by the rubric, without a verdict (R75 spec). A submission ends with feedback, "unreadable" — `blank` or `illegible` — or "unchecked", and the attempt stays open: the learner writes again and closes it themselves with the button "Done", open after at least one submission — and then the task is solved (R87 spec; П-19 closed). Feedback without words — they did not pass the leak check or the rule of the feedback — `app` shows with its own phrase "no feedback this time", not with the neutral one ([the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15). The marks of the criteria and their justifications are not on the device, and the adult sees the marks of the criteria in progress (R87 spec; Д83 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`).

**The task screen in the contract** — `AttemptService` (R97 spec; [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15; Д100 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`):
- a submission — `SubmitWork`, and for an essay `SubmitEssay`; the rung's words — `GetRungWords`, "explain differently" — `ExplainDifferently`, a hint — `GetHint`, a technique — `BeginStep`, showing the solution — `ShowSolution`, the correct answer words — `GetCorrectAnswerWords`, "Done" — `Done`;
- a refusal — the detail `Refusal` of a Connect error, and the AI call outcome — `Outcome` in the answer. A cause that the build does not know `app` reads by the error's code, and such an outcome as an AI failure;
- the request for the rung's words carries the number of the wrong answer, `wrong_answers` from the answer to the submission, and the submission's work: the server does not store the crop. The work of a correct submission `app` holds until the correct answer words, although "correct" closes the attempt (the second 2026-11-10 amendment to R04);
- a mark's name — by its kind, number and place (section 3.8), and `app` gives the marks to the canvas element by the property `marks` together with the submission context that it kept (R74 spec);
- the idempotency key — `idempotency_key` — is carried by the requests that must not be done twice: from the task screen `SubmitWork`, `SubmitEssay`, `GetRungWords`, `GetCorrectAnswerWords`, `ExplainDifferently` and `ShowSolution`, and also `ChooseNext`, `BeginAttempt`, `RequestChildConsent` and the PIN requests. `app` makes a key for each such action, and each repeat of it goes with the same key, while `GetHint`, `BeginStep` and `Done` carry no key and repeat by the state of the attempt. A repeat of what is done already gets what was recorded, without AI, and where the server does not store the words — for an essay, the rung's words and the correct answer words — an answer marked `repeated` and without words; the clock offset goes only with requests to platform AI (R118 spec). The client that keeps these rules is section 3.7, "Code", since T20 (R18).

**The step state** — the attempt's mode, the step's place, the ticks of the chips and the IDs of the techniques given — `app` keeps with the attempt in the profile database and erases with the end of the attempt, and for an unfinished one together with its work; an unfinished attempt is exported with it (section 3.2). After a relaunch the learner goes on in the same mode and from the step where they were. Each task of a lesson is an attempt of its own (architecture 3.5 spec) and begins from the first step.

**The path through the steps of the three modules of the first slice** — the steps from the format examples (`spec/format/examples/solving-steps/valid/`) on the tasks of concepts 16.1, 16.4 and 16.5 spec. No condition needs the key: `givens_ticked` looks at open fields, `answer_slots_filled` at `filled` and its own choice, `none` at nothing.

Olympiad maths, `olympiad-math.yaml`, the task `ladder.yaml` — a slot `anywhere`, two chips `givens`, `unknown`, and the techniques `draw` and `small-numbers` (concepts 16.1 spec):

| Step | Exit condition | Event on the device | What the tutor says | To `api` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Understand the task, `gives: [givens]` | `givens_ticked` | a tap on the chips "The rungs are 30 cm apart.", "It is 180 cm from the lowest rung to the highest." and "How many rungs there are." | the step's title, the chips, the line "Mark" | — |
| 2. Make a plan, `gives: [techniques]` | `none` | — | the title and the cards "Draw the problem" and "Start with small numbers" from the catalog | the start of step 2 — the answer: `draw`, `small-numbers` |
| 3. Solve it | `answer_slots_filled` | the first stroke on the sheet: `filled` is `[[true]]` | the title, the line "Write an answer" | — |
| 4. Check the answer | `none` | — | the title; the button is "Check" | on "Check" — the sheet's crop for reading (architecture 4.1 spec) |

School maths of grade 3, `school-math-grade-3.yaml`, the task `workbook-page.yaml` — five lines of the workbook and six boxes, without chips and techniques (concepts 16.4 spec):

| Step | Exit condition | Event on the device | What the tutor says | In `api` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Fill in the boxes | `answer_slots_filled` | a stroke in each box: `filled[item − 1][box − 1]` — the line "47 ÷ 6 = □ r □" has two boxes | the title, the line "write an answer" | — |
| 2. Check each line, `gives: [techniques]` | `none` | — | the title; the task has no techniques — there are no cards; the button is "Check" | the start of step 2 — the answer is empty; on "Check" — the crops of the filled boxes (R53 spec) |

Short essays in English, `short-essays.yaml`, the tasks `essay.yaml` — an answer slot `sheet` with a rubric — and `best-first-sentence.yaml` — an answer slot `choice` (concepts 16.5 spec). The module has one set of steps, so the task with a choice, too, goes through "Plan the essay" and "Write it":

| Step | Exit condition | Event on the device | What the tutor says | In `api` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Plan the essay, `gives: [techniques]` | `none` | — | the title; the example tasks have no techniques — there are no cards | the start of step 1 — the answer is empty |
| 2. Write it | `none` | — | the title | — |
| 3. Read it again | `none` | — | the title; the button is "Check", enabled when the sheet has a stroke (`sheet`) or an option is chosen (`choice`) | on "Check": for `essay`, the reading of the essay and the assessment by the rubric (R75 spec), for `best-first-sentence`, the value of the option, without AI (architecture 4.2 spec) |

The example tasks of the workbook and of the essays have no techniques, though the steps of these modules give them: such a step shows only its title. The step "pronounce" of the language steps will come with voice input (concepts 7 spec; О-6), and the state of a widget as a new exit condition, from stage 4 (architecture 3.4 spec).

**Where the prototype differs** ([the prototype's README](../spec/draft/README.md)): in it the answer is read on a pause — R69 spec rejected that; the steps go only forward; the step "Check" stands after the correct answer; the mode is set only by the adult in their section; the step "plan" has "Show me how to start" — format v0 has no sketch "how to start" (R57 spec). The documents take precedence.

**Code** — from T24, 2026-11-10 (R20):
- the entry — `src/task/`: the screen `#/task` opens an attempt whose ID is only in memory (`AttemptContext.tsx`): the open attempt outside a lesson that `GetAccount` named — "Continue the task" in "My subjects" — or a new one by "Try it again", `BeginAttempt` with `previous_attempt_id`. A new task by the engine choice is begun, from T28, by "Next task" or "What's next" (section 3.4, "Code"; R24), and a lesson's task by the lesson player, from T27 (section 3.4, "Code"): without them `BeginAttempt` has nothing to begin with (sources 7.5). Without an attempt, `#/task` leads home in its own place in the history — "Back" will not return here — and an ended one stays on the screen with its result until the learner leaves (`TaskRoute.tsx`). What the screen learned about the attempt — that it began or closed it — is kept with the profile that `GetAccount` gave, and after "Switch learner" too;
- reading — `Task.tsx`: `GetOpenAttempt` — the task's open view in the build's window of versions (`OpenViews.parseView`) and the package digest, by which the manifest, the solving steps and the technique catalog come from the shared cache; from the profile's database, the writing history and the state of the steps, and without a record on this device, a new record with a clean sheet (`resumed` in `attempt.ts`). The number of wrong answers is always the one that `api` holds; if it is not the one recorded here, there was a submission on another device, and the options are taken from the last submission; the step techniques that `api` holds fill the steps that have none recorded; and a verdict stands only at the option whose verdict it is. After the end of the attempt the screen writes nothing more. `CAUSE_ATTEMPT_CLOSED` — the screen "This task is already over" with the reason and "Try it again", the attempt's work on the device is erased, and for "unchecked" also `CheckPackages`; `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND` — home;
- the screen — `TaskScreen.tsx`, the author's layout: from 64rem, the canvas with the items under it on the left, the tutor's panel on the right, and "Check" as a row under them; narrower, one column: the panel, the canvas, the items, "Check". The row of "Check" does not stick to the bottom of the screen: it would cover the targets under it (R20). The mode switch is two buttons `aria-pressed` by the heading; in the mode "On my own" there is no panel until the task is solved;
- the items — `Items.tsx`: an item of the sheet is a `<fieldset>` with its text or "Item n", an answer slot `choice` is radio buttons, and an item with several answer slots has "Answer n". The verdict is at the chosen option, outside its caption and as the radio button's description, and at the item's text, by a word and a sign; the summary "Not right yet: n items of m" is `role="status"`; a changed option takes off the verdict of its answer slot and item;
- "Check" — enabled when every answer slot has something in it; disabled, it is `aria-disabled` with the line "Choose an answer in each item". `SubmitWork` — `tapped_choices` in the task's order, and the key is one per submission of these options and the same at its repeat. For a task with an answer slot that is not `choice`, "Check" is disabled until T25. "Correct" closes the attempt: the work on the device is erased, the panel gives way to the result — "Correct", the solution from `SolvedTask` (`Solution.tsx`: paragraphs and pictures by `blob:` with `alt`), "Try it again" and "Back to My subjects" — and the options can no longer be pressed;
- the steps — `steps.ts`, without the DOM, and `StepsPanel.tsx`: the state of the steps — the mode, the place, the farthest step, the ticks of the chips, the techniques by the step's place, the chosen options and the number of wrong answers — is in the attempt's record, and a foreign shape is read as its absence. The bar is an `<ol>` "Steps" with `aria-current="step"` and the word of the place; the step's title is an `h2`, and it takes the focus when the step changes; "Step done" is under `aria-disabled` and the line of the condition. `BeginStep` — once per step whose `gives` gives techniques, in the mode "by steps"; a failure asks again on the return to the step;
- the pause — `pause.ts`: one blink of "Check" — three flashes of 1 s each — after 6 s without a stroke and without a choice, if it is enabled, and again only after a new action;
- the hint, "Explain differently" and showing the solution — from T26 (R22): "Hint" is beside "Check", always; `GetHint` before the first wrong answer gives the techniques and the leading question, and the "Hint" block at the top of the panel (`Hint.tsx`) shows with them the "givens" and the "unknown" from the open view, and after it, the words of the rung once more, into the "Tutor" block; the screen takes what was given before a wrong answer, on continuing, from `requested_technique_ids` and `requested_leading_question`. "Explain differently" is a button of the "Tutor" block (`TutorWords.tsx`), `ExplainDifferently` with the number of the wrong answer, once per rung: the right is spent by an answer and by the refusals "already asked", "not current" and "no rung", while the quota, the limit and admission leave it. "Show the solution" — to a learner of the category `adult` from the start of the attempt, to a child from the fourth wrong answer, through the question "Once the solution is shown, the task is over"; `ShowSolution` as the learner's action — the result "Solution shown" with the solution, and "an adult is needed" and "the ladder is not passed" as a line;
- handwriting — from T25 (R21): "Check" for a task with handwriting is enabled by the element's `filled`, with the line "Write an answer on the sheet" while the sheet is empty; on it — `exportWork()`, the submission into the database with the context and the length of the history, and `SubmitWork` with the crop (`workOf` in `attempt.ts`) and the clock offset; a repeat of an unanswered one is the same key and the same work, and a new stroke is a new key. Under an item of handwriting — "Read: …" or the reason "unreadable" (`Items.tsx`), and for a workbook, a list of lines under the canvas; the AI call outcome is a line by "Check". "Not yet" at once calls `GetRungWords` with the same work: the words are the "Tutor" block at the top of the panel (`TutorWords.tsx`), with `lang`, and the line `role="status"` "The tutor's words came". While they are not there, the block stands in the mode "On my own" too, with the line "The tutor is writing…" — from T35: the rung's words by the model come ≈ 10 s after the verdict (row В54 `api`) — and for "Explain differently" the same line stands under the earlier words. The element gets the tutor's marks (`marksOf`) after rendering, with the submission's context, and the rejected ones are dropped. "Correct" — the work of the correct submission into `correctWork`, `GetCorrectAnswerWords` with it, and `wordsCame`. From T49 (R37) the "Tutor" block names the quota and the limit of platform AI by a line both under the rung's fallback text and after a correct answer without words, while an AI failure and "not admitted" give no line by the words. The line is one: the words of the same check and the refusal of "Explain differently" do not repeat the one said by "Check", and the model's words take it away. The quota says when it comes back — "today", "tomorrow" or a date by the device's calendar (section 3.8). After a restart the rung's words come from `OpenAttempt.rung`, and there are no tutor's marks: the open attempt does not carry them (`OpenAttempt` in `learning.proto`) — so it is on `api` too, in T35;
- tested in Chromium with the real canvas element (`Task.browser.test.tsx`): the steps, the chips and the technique, the mode and its record, the choice and "Check" from the keyboard, "Not yet" and "Correct" with the solution, the key of a repeat, a closed attempt, a restart — the mode, the place and the writing history — the continuation on another device, unsaved work and the blink. The manual check on a local `api` is T30.

### 3.4. The lesson player

Decided by R06. `app` leads a lesson itself, on the device, by the lesson's open view: the segments go in the author's order, and the engine does not reorder them (R58 spec; architecture 3.8 spec). In a lesson `app` gets no key: a worked example is not a task, it has neither answer slots nor a key, and the lesson's tasks go as ordinary attempts (section 3.3).

**Where a lesson comes from.**
- A lesson, or a task outside a lesson, is chosen by `engine` (architecture 3.8 spec). `api` gives the lesson whole, as its open view — `id`, `title` and the segments with all their fields, without skills and age categories — and only to a learner whom the lesson and each of its tasks suit; so the device part has no lessons (R58 spec and its 2026-10-28 amendment; R62 spec). Neither the lesson's title nor its texts contain the keys and the leak phrases of its tasks, after a task segment too (`lesson.no-leaks`; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21).
- With the lesson comes the digest of the package the lesson began on: all the lesson's tasks are attempts on this package, and a withdrawn version or a version outside the window ends the lesson "unchecked" (R07 api; sources 4.2). `app` takes the module manifest for the canvas element from the shared cache by this digest (R62 spec; section 3.2). In the contract the lesson is given by `OpenLesson` of `LearningService` — as a lesson run with its own ID on the package digest (R99 spec).
- The types are `Lesson`, `Segment`, `WorkedExample` and `DemoPart` from `@altnovatrail/spec/formatv0/open`. A segment is exactly one of three: `explanation`, `worked_example` or `task`. Neither a segment nor a part of the demo has an ID: a segment is named by its place in the lesson, from 1, and a part of the demo by its place in `demo` ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21).

**The lesson screen.**
- The header is on every segment: the lesson's title and the segment bar by places, with the kind of each — explanation, example or task — and with which are passed, which is current and which are not open yet. So the learner sees the lesson's title before each of its tasks too, as the format requires.
- Under the header is the segment itself, and at the bottom, "Continue" and the exit from the lesson.
- The strings of the header, the bar and the buttons are the interface: their language, rules and words are in section 3.8. The lesson's title is a text of the module, in the explanation language, with its own `lang`. The segment bar is a list where the current segment has `aria-current`, and a segment that does not open is `aria-disabled` with a line saying why (section 3.9).

**An explanation** is paragraphs and pictures, like the parts of a task statement ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14), and from format 0.4 also numbered lists, the part `numbered_list`: two texts or more in order, without numbers of their own ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21; R111 spec). `app` draws them itself, without the canvas element: a picture is an `<img>` with its `alt`, and a list is an `<ol>`, and the numbers 1, 2, 3 are put by it, not by the text (Д121 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`). `app` takes the picture's file from the device part that `api` serves, by the same `fetch` request as for the canvas, and shows it by a `blob:` address: the CSP does not let `<img>` reach `api` directly (section 3.7). The response lies in the browser's HTTP cache (section 3.2).

**A worked example** is a new element `<novatrail-canvas>` per segment, with a new `key` (section 3.1). From format 0.4 an example can also be on a workbook, with lines, like a task: the canvas shows it, and `app` gives it the same properties (R108 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21; Д121 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`). `app` gives it the attribute `lang`, then `manifest`, `workedExample` and, if this run has a saved demo state, `demoState` — in this order, because a state before the example is a `TypeError` (the second 2026-11-01 amendment to R36 spec).
- The parts of the demo are opened one at a time, and the whole demo is hidden and shown, by the learner, with the element's buttons "Next" and "Demo" (R42 canvas). A new demo begins with `{ opened: 0, hidden: false }`.
- The element sends the event `demochange` only on the learner's press. `app` writes its `detail` into the lesson state, by the example's place, and gives the element this same object, not a new literal (section 3.1).
- The learner does not write on the example's sheet: the example has neither answer slots, nor steps, nor "Check", and it does not serve as evidence (R58 spec).

**A lesson's task** is an attempt on the lesson's package, the same as outside a lesson (architecture 3.5 spec): the task screen of section 3.3 under the lesson's header, from the first step, with its own "Check" and hint. A lesson names a template as it names a task, and the learner gets one instance, by the seed of their attempt (the 2026-10-27 amendment to R58 spec; R60 spec), and the instance's open view comes with the attempt (section 3.2). When the task has ended — a correct answer, the showing of the task's solution or "unchecked" — is decided by `api`, by the hint ladder and the showing of the solution (architecture 3.5 spec; R82 spec).

**"Continue" and back.**
- "Continue" is the button of `app` to the next segment. It is not called "Next": that is the name of the element's button that opens a part of the demo (R42 canvas). "Continue" is a value of `app`'s own, not "next" of the icon table of architecture 6 spec (the third 2026-11-02 amendment to R78 spec), and its icon is `arrow-circle-right`: an arrow in a circle looks different from the arrow of "Next", `arrow-forward`, and from the chevron of "Demo", `keyboard-arrow-right`, and on the screen of a worked example all three are seen at once (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R06).
- On an explanation and an example "Continue" is enabled at once: the demo does not lock it. An example is neither a task nor evidence, and locking the button would mean putting pressure on the learner (product 7 spec), and they can return to the example.
- On a task "Continue" appears only when `api` has said that the task has ended — after the screen after the check (section 3.10). The next segment goes then, and not earlier ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21). There is no automatic move, as on the steps (section 3.3).
- Back is by a tap on the segment bar, to any passed explanation and example. An example opens as a new element with its own saved demo state. The place in the lesson does not change from this: "Continue" on a passed segment leads to the next passed explanation or example, skipping the segments of ended tasks, and after the last of them, to the place; the bar returns to the place at once.
- The bar shows the segment of a task that has ended as passed but does not open it: the attempt is closed, and solving the task again is a new attempt, and that is an engine choice. For a task that is not an essay, the work and the open view are erased besides (section 3.2). An essay with feedback by the rubric stays on the device but does not open from the lesson's bar: where the learner rereads it is decided by the epic E3, with the essay module (section 3.10).
- From an unfinished task the learner goes to an example or an explanation and comes back: the attempt has not ended, its work and the state of its steps lie in the profile's database (sections 3.2 and 3.3), and the task's new element gets `task` and `history`.

**The place in the lesson** is the segment the learner has reached. It moves on "Continue", and also when `api` has said that the task at the place has ended: the place moves to what comes next, though the learner still sees the screen after the check. So after a restart the lesson will not open on a task that has ended.

**Opening a lesson.** When `api` gives a lesson, there comes with it which of the lesson's attempts have ended and which has not, if there is one — the task of a segment (`OpenLesson`, and after a restart and on another device, `GetLessonRun` of `LearningService`; R99 spec). The lesson opens on the later of two places: its own, from the lesson state, and the one that comes after the last ended task by the answer of `api`. An unfinished attempt opens as the same attempt.
- Leaving in the middle of a lesson sends nothing: the lesson state lies in the profile's database until the term of 30 days without changes erases it (section 3.2). When `api` gives this lesson again on the same package, `app` opens it at the place and with the demos as they were.
- Another device: the lesson state lies only where the learner went through the lesson. On a device without the state the lesson opens after the last ended task, and if none has ended, on the first segment; the demos start from the beginning, and the passed segments before this place open back, as usual. An unfinished task opens as the same attempt, but with a clean sheet: the work lies on the device where it was written (section 3.2).

**The end of a lesson.**
- The usual end of a lesson is always "Continue" on the last segment, whatever its kind: then `app` tells `api` that the lesson is passed — `CompleteLesson` of `LearningService` — and repeats this until `api` answers. If `api` refused with `CAUSE_SEGMENTS_NOT_ENDED`, the refusal carries the places of the ended task segments, and the lesson opens on the first that has not ended (R99 spec; Д83 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`). If the last segment is a task, then at its end `api` answers that there are no more segments, and the learner presses "Continue", as after any task. Otherwise `api` would not learn that the learner read to the end the explanation after the last task, as in `gaps-and-posts.yaml`, and a lesson without tasks, like `reading-model-essays.yaml`, would never end (`spec/format/examples/lesson/valid/`). What `api` records about a passed lesson is decided by T12 `api`.
- Then `app` erases the lesson state and shows the end screen: the lesson's title, "Lesson complete" and a button to what the engine chose. It has no score, grade or comparison (product 7 spec), and the reason code of the choice is seen only by an adult (concepts 12 spec; R29 spec).
- `api` itself ends a lesson only in an unusual way, and then `app` does not send "lesson passed". There is no early end of a lesson in the first slice (R107 spec). If the lesson was ended "unchecked" — the version is withdrawn or outside the window (R63 spec; R07 api) —, "not suitable" — the module is disconnected, it has no current version for the learner, or the lesson no longer suits their age category — or "abandoned" — 30 days without requests —, a neutral phrase (R04 spec) and a button to the engine choice. The end of the run comes in an answer or as the refusal `CAUSE_RUN_ENDED` with its reason (R99 spec). In each case `app` erases the lesson state.

**Another version of the module.** Within a run the version does not change: the lesson holds the digest of its package (R07 api). `app` keeps the lesson state by the lesson's ID and the package digest, so the same lesson on another package is a new run: from the first segment and with the demos from the beginning. Beside the key it holds the run's ID, which `OpenLesson` gives, and drops the state of another run under the same key: the lesson on the same package could have ended and begun again (R99 spec; the second 2026-11-10 amendment to R06; Д83 and Д104 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`).
- `app` erases the earlier state as soon as it has learned that its run has ended: `api` ended the lesson for any reason — "unchecked", "not suitable", "abandoned" —, `app` learned of the withdrawal of the version (section 3.2), or `api` gave the same lesson on another package — then `app` erases the state of this lesson on all other digests before it opens the lesson. So a run that ended while the learner was on another device, or did not come in at all, does not lie on this one until the term of 30 days.
- Comparing examples by content is not needed, and the element would not make this comparison anyway: it checks a state only against the number of parts and takes one that fits by number silently, even if the parts are different (R42 canvas; sources 4.2). The state of another package never reaches the element.
- A record that the element would reject — `opened` not an integer from 0 to the number of the example's parts, or `hidden` neither `true` nor `false` — means a corruption of the database. `canvas` does not expose its own check (sources 4.2), so `app` checks these two fields itself: it does not give such a record to the element, and the demo begins from the start.

**The engine choice.**
- The next lesson or task is chosen by `engine`, and it does not reorder the lesson's segments (architecture 3.8 spec). A task outside a lesson is the task screen of section 3.3 without the lesson's header. The reason of the choice is not named to a child (concepts 12 spec).
- There is no consolidation within a lesson and no early end in the first slice: at the end of a segment `api` does not call the engine (R107 spec; R05 engine). `app` learns what comes next from the field `next` of the answer at the end of an attempt — the next segment, "no more segments" or the engine choice (R99 spec; Д104 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`). If consolidation comes, the contract will get a new reason, and `app` will take it with its own move to a tag.

**The mark "sequential"** — for a module whose skill graph is a chain (R58 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21):
- a chain — each skill of the module stands in its own tier, and the module has no fewer than two skills. Anchor skills give no tiers and are not counted; a module without a graph and with one skill has no mark. A redundant link — a skill needs both `a` and `b`, and `b` needs `a` — does not break the chain: the order of the skills does not change from it. So the 2026-11-02 amendment to R58 spec defines a chain;
- `app` does not compute the tiers itself: `module-kit` computes them when it builds the package, and `api` gives them to the skill map, `skill_tiers` ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 12 and 25). A module is a chain when each tier of `skill_tiers` has one skill. So the mark and the tiers of the skill map will not diverge: there is no second count;
- the tiers are needed before the first attempt too, for the current version of each connected module: `app` gets them as the open view of the skill graph with tiers — `GetSkillGraph` of `PackageService`, by the package digest of the module's current version (R99 spec; Д104 in table 4 of the RUN.md of `spec`);
- the mark is seen on the module's card in "My subjects" and on the module's page in the section for adults. It says that the skill graph puts the module's lessons in one row: a lesson comes after the lessons that teach the skills before its skills, and the engine chooses which of the lessons of one skill to give ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21). It is not in the lesson's header: it is about the order of lessons, not of segments. The words of the mark are the glossary of section 3.8. From T43 it is on the screen (R34): the line "In order" in `src/subjects/CardHeading.tsx` at each card of a module with a current version — in "My subjects", connected and in the catalog, in the list of skill maps and on the page of the profile's modules in the section for adults — and it is computed by `isSequential` in `src/subjects/modules.ts` by the `skill_tiers` of the graph's open view from the shared cache. The cards do not wait for the graphs: the mark goes onto a card when its graph has come — the hook `useSequential` in `src/subjects/` — and a graph that has not come gives no mark;
- in the format's examples the graph of olympiad maths is 17 skills in three tiers, and it is not a chain (`format/vectors/tiers/olympiad-math.yaml`), while the graph of the module `with-lessons` — two skills, `counting-gaps` and `time-calendar` after it — is a chain. Their graphs will tell whether the modules of the first batch have the mark, and `modules` writes them.

**The learner's path through a lesson of olympiad maths.** The lessons of the first batch do not exist yet — `modules` writes them —, so the path goes through the lesson `gaps-and-posts.yaml` from the format's examples (`spec/format/examples/lesson/valid/`) — "A lesson of the official module of olympiad mathematics". The lesson teaches the skill `counting-gaps`, and in the official module its task `gaps-ladder-1` is the ladder task `task/valid/ladder.yaml` (concepts 16.1 spec), with an answer slot `anywhere`, chips and the steps of `olympiad-math.yaml` (section 3.3). In the examples module `with-lessons` the same lesson lies with a short task `gaps-ladder-1` — without chips, techniques and steps: this module has no steps, and the task there is solved as in the mode "self".

| Segment | On the screen | Event on the device | In the profile's database | In `api` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Explanation | the header "Gaps and posts", a bar of four segments; a paragraph about posts and gaps and the picture `fence-posts-and-gaps.png` with its `alt` | "Continue" — enabled at once | the lesson with the package digest; the place is 2 | the lesson came from `api` together with the digest |
| 2. Worked example | a new element: the statement "A path is 20 m long…", a sheet with a grid, the parts closed — `{ opened: 0, hidden: false }` | "Next" three times: `demochange` — `{ opened: 1, hidden: false }`, a paragraph; `{ opened: 2, … }`, the picture `path-in-gaps.png` in the sheet area `left: 100, top: 150`, 800 × 200; `{ opened: 3, … }`, a paragraph with the answer. "Demo" — `{ opened: 3, hidden: true }`. "Continue" is enabled all the time | the demo state of place 2 — after each `demochange`; the place is 3 | — |
| 3. Task `gaps-ladder-1` | the task screen under the lesson's header, from the first step: "Understand the task", the chips, the steps up to "Check the answer" (section 3.3) | strokes, chips, "Check"; back to the example and back again — the demo `{ opened: 3, hidden: true }`, the task's work in place | the attempt: the writing history, the submissions, the state of the steps (section 3.2); at the end of the task the place is 4 | an attempt on the lesson's package; reading and verdict; `api` says that the task has ended — a correct answer or the showing of the solution — and that next comes the next segment |
| 4. Explanation | "Each time, count the gaps first…"; segment 3 on the bar is passed and does not open | "Continue" on the last segment | the lesson state is erased | "lesson passed"; then the engine choice |

After segment 4 — the end screen: "Gaps and posts", "Lesson complete" and a button to what the engine chose. In the format's examples module `with-lessons`, where the same lesson lies, the lesson `days-and-dates` comes after it by the graph: its skill `time-calendar` stands after `counting-gaps`.

- **A restart** on segment 3: `app` finds the lesson state by the ID `gaps-and-posts` and the digest and opens segment 3 — the attempt with the writing history and the state of the steps. The example on segment 2 opens with `{ opened: 3, hidden: true }`, as it was left.
- **A change of the module's version:** the version on which the lesson began was withdrawn. `api` ends the lesson "unchecked" (R07 api), `app` shows a neutral phrase and erases the lesson state. When the engine gives `gaps-and-posts` again, now on the new version, the key — the lesson's ID and the new digest — finds nothing: the lesson goes from the first segment, and the demo from `{ opened: 0, hidden: false }`. Had the new version's example two parts, the element would reject the earlier `{ opened: 3, hidden: true }` with a `TypeError`, and had it four, it would take it silently, with other parts; neither happens, because the element does not get the earlier state.
- **Another device:** the task of segment 3 ended on a computer, and the learner opened the lesson on a tablet. There is no state there, and `api` says that the task of segment 3 has ended: the lesson opens on segment 4, and the example on segment 2 from the start of the demo.

**Where the prototype differs** ([the prototype's README](../spec/draft/README.md)): the mark *Sequential* stands at module packages and leads them "lesson by lesson in the author's order", while by R58 spec it is derived from the graph, and the next lesson is chosen by `engine`; the prototype has no demo (T31 in the RUN.md of `spec`). The documents take precedence.

**Code** — from T27, 2026-11-10 (R23):
- the entry — `src/lesson/`: the screen `#/lesson` opens a lesson run whose ID is only in memory (`LessonContext.tsx`): the runs begun, from `open_lesson_runs` of `GetAccount` — the block "A lesson in progress" in "My subjects" with "Continue the lesson" — through `GetLessonRun`, and by an engine choice of a lesson, `OpenLesson`, by "Next lesson" or "What's next" from T28 (R24). The block names the run by the lesson's title from its state on the device, with the module's `lang`, and without the state, "A lesson: {module}", where the module's name is a placeholder element with its own `lang` (section 3.8). Without a chosen run, `#/lesson` goes home in its own place in the history (`LessonRoute.tsx`); a run that has ended leaves the list after "Switch learner" too;
- reading — `Lesson.tsx`: the lesson's open view in the build's window of versions, the manifest from the shared cache by the digest, and the lesson state from the profile's database — by the lesson's full ID (the module ID of the manifest, `lesson` and the lesson's ID; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10), the digest and the run's ID; `readLesson` erases a record of another digest or run (R16). The place is `openingPlace` in `place.ts`, without the DOM: the later of its own, the one after the last ended task, and the segment of the open attempt, past ended tasks, except for the last segment. The lesson state is written on opening, at each move of the place and after each `demochange`, and a write that did not go through is the line "Your work isn't saved on this device";
- the screen — `LessonScreen.tsx` and `SegmentBar.tsx`: the `h1` is the lesson's title with the module's `lang` (`Screen` takes the heading as a node); the bar "Segments of the lesson" is an `<ol>` of chips 44 × 44 with a number, wrapping to a second line and with the sign ✓ of a passed one, and from 64rem also with the word of the kind; a chip's name is the number and the kind, its text, and "passed", "now", "later" or why the segment does not open is its description. A press on a chip that does not open is a line of the screen with the chip's name: "5 Task: not open yet". From T35 it is so, because a bare "Not open yet" under the heading of the segment shown read as said about that segment. Under the bar is the `h2` "Worked example · 2 of 4", to which the focus goes when the segment changes; at the bottom, "Continue" with the icon `continue` and "Leave the lesson";
- the explanation — `Explanation.tsx`: paragraphs, pictures — the shared `src/packages/Picture.tsx`, the same as for a task's solution — and `numbered_list` as an `<ol>`. The example — `Example.tsx`: a new element per segment, `lang`, the manifest, `media`, `workedExample` and `demoState` in this order, and `demochange` writes `detail` into the state by the place and gives the element the same object; a record that the element would reject is not given (`demoOf` in `run.ts`);
- the lesson's task — `BeginAttempt` with the run's segment and a key, the same at a repeat, when the learner has reached the segment, and the open attempt at the place comes from `LessonRun.open_attempt`. `Task.tsx` in a lesson is without its own screen, "Try it again" and "Back to My subjects" (`TaskInLesson`), and the `next` of the answer to `SubmitWork` and `ShowSolution` goes to the player: `segment` moves the place at once, `segments_ended` leaves it on the last segment, and an empty one means a repeated `GetLessonRun`. An attempt closed "left" or "abandoned" gives "Begin the task again", and an attempt that `api` does not know, a repeated `GetLessonRun` and a new attempt of the segment. The refusal `CAUSE_SEGMENT_UNAVAILABLE` leads on if the segment has ended by itself, and otherwise to the first task that has not ended, even if it is before the place; a refusal without places means a repeated `GetLessonRun`. The quota, the limit, "not admitted" and an answer without an attempt are a line of the task segment, with the run open;
- the end — `CompleteLesson` on "Continue" on the last segment, again on "Continue" after a failure; `CAUSE_SEGMENTS_NOT_ENDED` — the first task segment that has not ended, with the line "The lesson has a task to finish". The end of the run for any reason — by an answer or by `CAUSE_RUN_ENDED` at opening, at the start of a segment, at a repeated reading and at "lesson passed" — erases the lesson state; "unchecked" also calls `CheckPackages`. The end screen is `LessonEnd`: the lesson's title, "Lesson complete" or the phrase of the reason (`endKey` in `run.ts`) and "Back to My subjects";
- the engine choice — from T28 (R24), `src/next/`: `NextStep.tsx` — "Next lesson" or "Next task" by the choice from the answer about the end of an attempt outside a lesson (`next.choice` of `SubmitWork` and `ShowSolution`) or of a run (`RunEnd.choice` of `CompleteLesson` and in the refusal `CAUSE_RUN_ENDED`), and without it, "What's next", `ChooseNext`; "nothing to give" and a choice of an unknown kind (`givenOf` in `choice.ts`) — the line "There is nothing more to offer for now" with the outcome. A lesson by the choice is the player, `OpenLesson`; a task is `BeginAttempt` with `choice_id`, and the refusal `CAUSE_ALREADY_EXISTS` gives the open attempt from `GetAccount`. "My subjects" has "What's next" without an open attempt and "Something else" beside "Continue the task". `ChooseNext` and the start of a task by the choice close the attempt open outside a lesson: before them there is a question, wherever the button stands, and after them the attempt's work on the device is erased (`attemptOver` in `src/storage/profile.ts`, shared with the task screen). `#/task` takes the attempt begun from it itself, and `#/lesson` the new chosen lesson; tested in Chromium — `NextStep.browser.test.tsx`, the results of a task and of a lesson, and the path through `App`;
- tested in Chromium with the real canvas element (`Lesson.browser.test.tsx`): the lesson's path above — an explanation with a picture and a list, the example and its demo in the database, the task, the explanation, "Lesson complete" — a restart on segment 3, another device, another run under the same key and a corrupted demo record, an example on a workbook and the end by `segments_ended`, an empty `next`, showing the solution, a task abandoned and one not begun, each end of a run; the entry is through `App` (`AccountGate.browser.test.tsx`). The manual check on a local `api` is T30.

### 3.5. Voicing

Decided by R07. `app` voices the tutor's words with the device's speech synthesis — `speechSynthesis` from the Web Speech API (sources 5.1). Voicing only repeats the text: it always stays on the screen. The boundary with `tutor` is decided by R56 tutor: the same text that passed the checks of `api` is voiced, the model writes no separate text for speech, and `tutor` takes no part in voicing by the device (sources 5.1). Voicing is for everyone: it is output, not a collection of data (privacy 6 spec), as long as the text does not leave the device. So `app` chooses the voice itself, below.

**When.** Voicing is in the first slice, stage 2, and its code comes after the first app and before the first child: the first app does not name it (R76 spec). product 10 spec names it under "In" (R12). Voicing by an AI provider and speech recognition wait for stage 3, and speech-to-speech for the tutor's words is closed (Т-13; R56 tutor). Voicing by the device needs no real-time channel: the tutor's words come whole (R54 spec; Т-25). In canvas-api voicing stood after the MVP (sources 10.1).

**What is voiced.**
- By itself, if the learner's voicing is on: the tutor's words that come from `api` — the rung's question, a hint, feedback by a rubric or a fallback text (architecture 4.1 spec; R70 spec). With them, the lines of the tutor's panel: the verdict, the reason "unreadable" and the neutral phrase (section 3.3). Voicing begins when the whole text is already on the screen: the tutor's words come whole, once the whole text has passed the leak check and the form check (R54 spec, R70 spec).
- By the button with a speaker beside the text — always, when there is a voice for its language, and with voicing off too:
  - a repeat of the tutor's words;
  - the task statement — paragraphs, items, lines of a workbook and the options of `choice`;
  - the chips, the step's title and the technique card (section 3.3);
  - the paragraphs of a lesson's explanation and the opened parts of a worked example, except pictures (section 3.4).

  These are the module's open texts, which the learner reads anyway (R05 spec).
- Not voiced are the buttons and captions of the interface and the `alt` of pictures: a screen reader reads them, and how `app` works with it is in section 3.9. `app` does not voice the learner's work, except their words that feedback by a rubric quotes (R70 spec).
- `app` does not call the button "listen": concepts 8 spec keeps that word for an action of the text canvas. The words of the button and of the lines of voicing follow the rules and the glossary of section 3.8: the button is "Read aloud", without gender.

**Language.** Each text sounds in its own language:
- the module's texts and the tutor's words — in the module's explanation language, `languages.explanation` of the manifest: a module has one language, as a BCP 47 tag (R46 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 11);
- the strings of `app` — in the interface language (section 3.8): on the learner's screens, the language of their profile, and before the choice of the learner and in the section for adults, the device's language.

Voices are needed for both, and by the recommendation of О-2 these are English and Russian. The example manifests of the three modules of the first slice have the explanation language `en` (`spec/format/examples/manifest/valid/`), and there is no target language in the slice: it has no language modules (R41 spec). If there is no voice for the language of a text, only that text is silent.

**Which voice.** `app` always names the voice itself — the `voice` of each `SpeechSynthesisUtterance` — and leaves the browser no choice. Without a named voice the specification tells the browser to take its default voice, and it "can be a local or remote speech service". Chrome picks such a voice only by language, does not prefer a local one, and for `ru` it has the network voice «Google русский» (sources 5.1). The voice for a language L:
1. The candidates are the voices from `getVoices()` whose `localService` is true, whose name has no "Online" and whose `lang` matches L. First the same tag matches, then a tag that begins with L — `en-US` and `en-GB` to `en`: this is the basic filtering of RFC 4647, "Basic Filtering". If there are none, the same for L without its last subtag: `pt-PT` too will match `pt-BR`. Case does not matter, and `_` in `lang` is read as `-`: Chrome on Android gives `en_US`. The explanation language in the example manifests is `en`, without a region, while the voices' `lang` has a region, like `ru-RU` of «Google русский» (sources 5.1).
2. The voice that the adult chose for L on this device, if it is among the candidates.
3. Otherwise the candidate with `default`, if the language has one such, otherwise the first in the list. WebKit sets `default` on every voice (sources 5.1), so in Safari, without the adult's choice, the first one the list gives is taken. Д5 checks on devices which voice turns out first, and the adult sees the voice and changes it on the page "This device".
4. Otherwise there is no voice for L.

`app` reads the list of voices at launch and rereads it on `voiceschanged`: in Chrome the list does not come at once, network voices disappear without a network, and the adult can add a voice in the system (sources 5.1).

**What `localService` knows** is only what the browser knows, not the system (sources 5.1; architecture 8 spec). So Т-13 recorded it, and voicing on Android and Linux with the adult's permission is a choice of the system that the adult makes, not a disclosure (R118 spec; П-22 closed).

| Platform | What `localService` says | What `app` does |
|---|---|---|
| Chrome on a computer | `false` for the network voices of Google — "Google US English", «Google русский»: Chrome sends their text to `www.google.com/speech-api`. These voices are only in Google's Chrome, and not in ChromeOS. `true` for the system's voices | takes the system's voices |
| Edge | voices with "Online" in the name are synthesized by Azure, and the text goes to Microsoft; the others, on the device, "no information is sent to Microsoft servers" | does not take a voice with "Online" in the name, even if `localService` is true |
| Safari | `true` for every voice: WebKit gives only the voices of Apple's system and sets `default` on each | takes the system's voices; all have `default`, so the adult's choice comes first |
